## 2026-11-14: The start-up capture panic — not reproduced in 44 start-only launches; narrowed to an info-cache miss inside a capture

Status: deferred (no code landed; a diagnostic is saved).

Question: backlog item 7 (one start in ~35 on 2026-11-13 panicked in the warm-up's graph captures: no room for a
56-byte buffer while a capture records).

- 44 start-only launches of the three-slot 256k server on chunk128 with `RUST_BACKTRACE=1` (`chain-x16.sh`, four
  batches of 11): 0 panics. (Batch a's logged panic count is a script bug, a shell variable reused by the kill
  helper; no panic log was kept in any batch.) About 35 more starts in today's experiments: none.
- Mechanism, from `third_party/cubecl-cuda/src/compute/server.rs` (`info_buffer`): a launch's info words are staged
  in a device buffer cached by their contents; a launch whose words are not in the cache allocates one, and an
  allocation that needs a new pool page is refused while a capture records. 56 bytes is a seven-word info set.
- Diagnostic (`QX_INFO_MISS_LOG=1`, chunk130d: a line per info set first staged while a capture records): one
  start, 0 misses. A normal start allocates nothing inside its captures, so the panic needs a launch whose info
  words differ between a width's dry pass and its capture, or an entry evicted between the two. Which launch: not
  found (the window path's launches with host-dependent arguments were not all read).

Next: carry the diagnostic line unconditionally in the next build that is validated anyway (it prints only on such
a miss), so the next occurrence names the words. Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-14/x-startup-panic/`.

## 2026-11-14: A deep rewind replays through one trimmed chunk, and a prefix it cannot pay for is not reused — LANDED, default on (2,047 tokens with 1,252 shared: 28.5 -> 4.5 s at the client; 12.5k with 11.7k shared: 7.5 s against 10.9 afresh; exact mode 97.6 -> 14.2 s)

Status: landed, default on; not a promotion (the baseline stays 19.166).

Question: the user asked for automatic prefix caching like vLLM's (backlog item 2 of the morning queue). Measuring
today's reuse first found it broken for a prefix that ends more than 128 tokens (`ring_w - n_swa`) before the slot's
end: `rewind` then rebuilds the rings by replaying up to `replay_span` = 5,208 tokens in uncaptured windows, about
20 ms per token, and the server's prompt timer started after the rewind, so no log showed it.

Before (three-slot 256k server, client-side seconds, 16-token replies):
- chunk128 (X19 / X20): 2,047 tokens, 1,252 shared with the slot's 1,638-token prompt: 28.5 s (the server logged
  2.37 s); afresh 5.16 s.
- The windows' replay on chunk132 (`QX_DS_REPLAY_CHUNK=0`, X22 w), exact mode: 12,528 tokens, 11,734 shared, 97.6 s
  (the 12,785-token prompt before it took 22.8 s afresh; the 12,528-token one was not run afresh in exact mode).
- A prefix ending within the last 128 tokens (the same prompt again, a continuation) is free: 0.72 s (X26 def / X28
  def, chunk133 / chunk134).

Change (`rewind2.diff`, sha256 fec60138f36f, on 9bb819c; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk134`: qx-ds-check 0aa49a4ee1c5,
quixiinfer 188c1688b3cf, qx-ds-multi 58868a9c9863, qx-ds-replay e71d350ed6b7; chunk133 = the same engine tools,
its server without the last two points; chunk132 = the same engine tools, its server with a threshold of 10,000,
as chunk131's: no test tells 8,192 from 10,000, every default-arm prefix being 1,252, 10,728 or free):
1. `replay_to` runs the replay through the chunk path (`forward_chunk_inner` with `replaying` set; the guard on the
   store and index-key writes is in `layer_attn`, which both paths share) when the engine has chunk buffers and the
   replay is 64+ tokens. `QX_DS_REPLAY_CHUNK=0`: windows.
2. In a replay chunk layer l runs its last `n_swa + (n_layer - l) * (n_swa - 1)` rows only, from a slab boundary
   (the layers below a ring window need 127 more rows each; one layer's worth of margin, as `replay_span` has).
   `QX_DS_REPLAY_TRIM=0`: every row through every layer.
3. `rewind_fast`; the scheduler reuses a prefix of 256+ tokens only when the rewind is free or the prefix has
   `QX_DS_REWIND_DEEP_MIN` tokens (default 8,192); else the prompt runs afresh.
4. Among free slots sharing equally, the one holding the fewest tokens (ties went to the last in the list: a
   145-token prompt reset the slot holding a 12.8k prompt while two were empty: X26 d).
5. The request's clock starts before the rewind.

Exactness of the replay (exact kernels, `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`):

| test | result |
|---|---|
| X25 c2: `qx-ds-check`, 560 single steps, rewind to 400 by a 400-row replay chunk, positions 400.. again | 160 / 160 rows bit-identical |
| X25 c1: 1,280 steps, rewind to 1,024 (replay from 0, top layers trimmed) | 256 / 256 |
| X25 b: 5,632 steps, rewind to 5,376 (the full 5,208-row trimmed replay, at 168) | 256 / 256 (restore 9.05 s) |
| X22: server, 12,528 tokens with 11,734 shared, trimmed chunk / untrimmed chunk / windows | reply (48 tokens), the 794-row stretch chunk's last-row hash and 63 single-row steps (a warm-up row, 15 decode rows of the first reply, 47 after the deep reuse) equal in the three arms; 14.2 / 21.6 / 97.6 s |
| X26 a: server, 10,739 tokens with 10,728 shared (the suffix in windows after the replay) against the same prompt afresh (`QX_DS_PREFIX=0`) | reply the same; the last prompt row's hash (`92a95224d6b47a33`) and all 47 decode rows equal |
| X28 d (chunk134): the same deep reuse while another slot decodes a 93-token reply | the reuse reply equals the fresh arm's (`x26-a-fresh.txt`, chunk133); the decoder's reply equals `x12-t3-off.X.txt` (the same prompt on chunk128 at 07:34, two other requests on that server). The 11-token suffix ran in a 12-wide window with the decoder's row. The decoder's 93 tokens took 14.46 s, 8.25 s of it the replay chunk: a deep rewind runs at admission and stalls the other slots for the whole replay (it is not a chunk step; beside the same prompt afresh, X26 d, the decoder took 25.22 s) |

Before this change `replay_to` had no standing check (ds-checks line 5 rewinds 64 of 128: the free path; the gate's
states unpark with their rings). X25 is the first; it is not yet in ds-checks.

Default mode, a numerics change: a deep rewind's replay was exact (windows) and is f16 now for 256+ rows, like any
prompt chunk. Reference comparison (X32 kl, `qx-ds-check` of chunk136 = the worktree build: this engine, the cross-slot copy
function this path does not call, and a KL print in the check; 1,280 single steps, rewind to 1,024 through an f16
replay chunk, the 256 rows after it against the straight exact pass): KL mean 0.0063, max 0.129, top-1 249 / 256
(97.3%); 0 of 256 bit-identical. One shape: a 1,024-row replay at 1k context; the 5,208-row replay has no KL figure. Replies after a deep reuse
against the same prompt afresh: 16 tokens, four prefixes (X23, texts compared afterwards with `cmp`: 88, 88, 86, 95
bytes) and one more (X27): the same in all five; 48 tokens after the 10,728-token reuse (X32, chunk134, defaults):
the texts part at byte 81 of 223 (the reuse arm's suffix runs in exact windows after an f16 replay, the afresh arm
is two f16 chunks, 7,943 rows KV-only and 2,796; both replies read as the same summary). Both differ from the
exact-mode reply to that prompt (`x26-a-fresh.txt`, 227 bytes): the reuse arm from byte 81, the afresh arm from byte
137. Server-side times of the pair: prompt 5.03 s + reply 2.75 s against 9.24 + 2.98 (at the client 7.83 against
12.27 s, not like for like: the afresh arm is a new server's first request).

Where deep reuse pays (default mode, `QX_DS_REWIND_DEEP_MIN=256` against never; client seconds; chunk132, X23, one
run per point; the two 7,052 rows chunk133, X27 and X26 thr; the slot's tiers as the previous prompt left them):

| prefix shared | prompt | reuse | afresh |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 1,252 | 2,047 | 6.13 | 4.56 |
| 2,684 | 3,478 | 6.43 | 4.99 |
| 2,684 | 6,442 | 8.97 | 6.94 |
| 5,678 | 6,471 | 7.44 | 6.84 |
| 7,052 | 8,957 | 8.75 / 8.49 | 8.95 / 8.81 |
| 7,052 | 12,785 | 11.31 | 11.11 |
| 11,734 | 12,528 | 7.52 | 10.92 |

The replay chunk is ~4.4 s whatever the prefix (5,208 rows) and the stretch after it a chunk of its own; afresh
grows ~0.67 ms per token. The threshold is bracketed, not measured: 5,678 shared loses 0.6 s, 7,052 is a tie
inside the repeats' spread (and loses 0.2 s with a long suffix), 11,734 wins 3.4 s, nothing between 7,052 and
10,728. 8,192 sits on the safe side of the tie.

With the defaults (chunk134, X28 def): 12,785 tokens 11.93 s; the same again 0.72 s; 10,739 tokens with 10,728
shared 6.14 s (the server now logs 5.01 s for the prompt: the replay is inside); a 1,638-token prompt goes to an
empty slot; 2,047 tokens with 1,252 shared with that one 4.46 s afresh (28.5 s before).

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-14): HOLD, then LANDED, default on, not a promotion
The hold: one identity session on chunk134's server, no `json_object` request, no reference comparison for the
default-mode numerics change. Lifted by `x32-serve256k-2-180036` (identical), X33 (the same response on chunk134 and
chunk128) and X32 kl (KL 0.0063, top-1 97.3%, inside 0.03 / 95%). The reply that parts from the afresh one in
default mode is inside the contract, which bounds the distribution, not the text: both default arms differ from the
exact reply, reuse differed from afresh on chunk128 too (X19), and under `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1` reuse and afresh
are equal down to the row hashes. Gate 19.166 -> 19.177 (+0.1%), decode256k at the level of every run since
2026-11-13; no scored case rewinds deep; the win is on the secondary metric (28.5 -> 4.46 s; 97.6 -> 14.2 s, single
runs). Flags: the next experiment must attack a presupposition (none of the last five did); serve3 not run on
chunk134; no test tells a threshold of 8,192 from 10,000. Nineteen text corrections over the two passes, applied.

## 2026-11-14: Prefix reuse across slots — LANDED, default on (a 12.8k prompt arriving beside its first copy: 15.79 -> 0.08 s of prompt time, 4.6 s once the source is 128+ tokens into its reply; a slot's rings are tracked as exact or not)

Status: landed, default on (`QX_DS_PREFIX_COPY=0`: off); not a promotion (the baseline stays 19.166). The breaker
found a defect in the first form, fixed and retested below.

Question: the user asked for automatic prefix caching like vLLM's. After today's deep-rewind landing a request
reuses a prefix only from a free slot. Nothing is shared with a running slot: three clients with one long system
prompt compute it three times.

Change (`copy2.diff`, sha256 20083f547fd1, on 8451d2c; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk137`: qx-ds-check 2c0210dee288,
quixiinfer 69758469cd8e, qx-ds-multi 093f5d6512a0, qx-ds-replay bc700bf29352):
1. `copy_prefix(src, dst, upto)`: the source slot's compressed-store rows below `upto`, its rings and its tokens,
   device to device (`qx_ds_copy_f32`), then `rewind(dst, upto)`. The source goes on undisturbed.
2. `exact_from` per slot: the position from which a rewind finds every layer's ring window exact (0 after a reset;
   a KV-only chunk or window ending at q makes it q + `prompt_keep` - `n_swa`; a replay to p makes it p; a copy
   carries the source's; an unpark makes it the parked length). `rewind` and `rewind_fast` take the free path only
   at or above it. In the server no rewind that was free becomes a replay (a finished prompt leaves it at its end
   less 128 or lower). An `unpark` below the parked length now always replays (only `qx-ds-check` parks; the gate's
   states unpark at their own length); `park` does not clear it (conservative); a ring restore followed by a free
   rewind below the parked length has no standing check any more; the comment in `unpark` about the fast path is
   stale.
3. The scheduler values a prefix in tokens of prompt work saved: its length when the rewind is free, its length
   less `QX_DS_REPLAY_COST` (6,500) when it needs a replay and has `QX_DS_REWIND_DEEP_MIN` tokens, nothing
   otherwise. The best free slot by value (then fewest tokens), and a running slot's prefix copied into it when it
   is worth 256+ tokens more. `QX_DS_PREFIX_COPY=0`: no copies. The 6,500 is the replay's ~4.4 s over the ~0.67 ms
   per token of a prompt afresh, not swept. Untested branches: two free slots that both hold a prefix, and a copy
   into a free slot that holds a shorter prefix. In the gate's serve sessions every slot is worth 0.
4. `qx-ds-replay` runs the `P src dst upto` trace line; `qx-ds-check`'s rewind check prints KL and top-1 too.

The breaker's finding (fresh context, on the first form, `copy.diff` / chunk136): a source still inside its prompt
has passed KV-only chunks, whose rows never wrote the rings above the last KV source; the scheduler found
`rewind_fast` true at the source's read position, and the copy took those rings as they were. X36 (exact mode;
A = 12,785 tokens, B = the same text cut to 8,741 tokens, sent 2 s later and admitted after A's first chunk:
`P 0 d 8192`): on chunk136, 0 of B's 47 decode rows equal the copy-off arm's (the 48-token reply happened to be
the same text); on chunk137, 47 / 47, B's last prompt row's hash (`54244f37bc454cff`) and all 461 of the source's
traced rows (the adjudicator's recount; my first count, 36 and 20, took the single-row steps only). Times of X36,
exact mode: B's prompt 22.56 s against 27.78 s without the copy, the source's 29.65 against 29.17 s (the 8.24 s
replay at admission is inside both). The same hole was reachable before today without
any copy: a slot left inside its prompt and rewound within the ring's reach (no way to leave one was found in
the server: a cancellation is seen only at the first emitted token); `exact_from` closes both.

Exactness on chunk137 (`QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`):

| test | result |
|---|---|
| X36: a copy from a source between its chunks (deep: replay) against no copy | B's 47 decode rows, its last prompt row and the source's 461 rows equal; replies equal |
| X38: a 12.8k prompt decoding 100 tokens; the same prompt arrives (free copy) | its 48-token reply equals the same-slot repeat's and the afresh one's (48 / 48 and 47 / 47 rows); the source's first bytes equal the afresh 48-token reply |
| X39 deep: a prompt sharing 11,734 tokens arrives beside the decoding source (copy, replay) | its reply equals the same-slot deep reuse's (`x22-t.7.txt`); the source's unchanged |
| X39 replay: `qx-ds-replay` on both traces | 121 and 122 forwards, 0 mismatching rows (determinism of `P`, not correctness: the cross-arm rows above are the correctness evidence) |

Times, default mode, three-slot 256k server, one run per arm (chunk137; the same on chunk136, X30):

| case (X37, X40) | copy | no copy |
|---|--:|--:|
| the same 12,785-token prompt arriving while the first is ~45 tokens into its reply: prompt time | 0.08 s | 15.79 s |
| ... its wall time (48 tokens) | 10.75 s | 25.47 s |
| ... the first client's 100 tokens, wall | 24.66 s | 38.91 s |
| a prompt sharing 11,734 tokens with it (replay): prompt time | 6.87 s | 16.68 s |
| the same 12,785-token prompt once the source is 128+ tokens into its reply (replay): prompt time / wall | 4.56 / 6.79 s | 10.41 / 12.81 s |
| the same 6,442-token prompt, source 128+ tokens in (under the deep threshold: not copied) | 6.08 s | 6.08 s |

The free copy holds only while the source is within 128 tokens of the shared point; after that a prefix of 8,192+
tokens costs the replay and a shorter one is computed afresh.

Default mode, disclosed: the copied request's last prompt token runs in an exact window where the afresh arm's is
an f16 chunk row, as for a same-slot repeat. So two identical prompts sent together at temperature 0 can now give
different replies (X37: replies 1 and 2 part at "provided" / "pasted", byte 58; without the copy they agree;
replies 1 and 3 are byte-equal with and without the copy), where before
they were computed twice the same way. Within the contract (the distribution is bounded, not the text) and equal
under `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`, but it is a dependence on company of the kind 2026-11-13 removed for arrival beside
a decoder: for the adjudicator to rule on.

Breaker's other notes: the gate cannot see a copy (its prompts are under 256 tokens); a copy's replay runs at
admission and stalls the other slots outside the chunk gap; the copy's cost at 250k tokens (~2.5 GiB device to
device) is unmeasured; the first form chose by length, not cost, and took the emptiest slot even when the chosen
one held a redundant prefix (both changed in point 3; no test of either yet).

Single-part chunks and the gate (chunk137): tail dumps at 2,048 and 16,384 tokens byte-identical to chunk134's;
ds-checks parts a and b the same text as v11's (tokenizer line included; line 5's park / unpark to 64 of 128 now
replays, still 64 / 64); set D `setd-promo33-191126` 19.144 against 19.166 (decode256k 38.8 / 38.8, prefill256k 2.0,
serve256k1 39.68, serve256k3 90.42, decode 30.7, prefill128 10.5; identity serve256k-1 identical); a second
serve256k session `x41-serve256k-2-191900`: 39.26 / 90.99, identical. Preflight 19:11:26: VM shut off, 3,377 MiB
used, swap 1,028 used, pressure avg10 0.00, no build tools, diff 20083f547fd1; X36-X40 wrote none (the journal:
VM off since 01:03, builds at 18:19:48 and 18:34:44 only, no concurrent servers). No scored case copies (`prefix
copies 0` in both serve sessions). Also on chunk137: a `json_object` request (X43: the message equal to
chunk134's); the same-slot shapes of X28 (X42 def: 0.73 s for the repeat, 6.14 s for the 10,728-token deep reuse,
empty slots taken; X42 d: every traced row equal to X28 d's, 519 and 93); the rewind check of X32 (X43: default
mode KL 0.0063, top-1 249 / 256; exact mode 256 / 256 bit-identical). Not run: `serve1` / `serve3` (not in
`--promo`); a copy beyond 12.8k tokens (at 250k it moves ~2.5 GiB device to device).

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-14/x-prefix-copy/` (copy.diff = the first form, copy2.diff, chain scripts, log.txt,
server-log extracts, x36-trace-comparisons.txt); traces in `~/qx-chains` (x36-*, x38-*, x39-*).

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-14): LANDED, default on, not a promotion
Gate 19.166 -> 19.144 (-0.1%), nothing scored copies. Row-level exactness on the final binary recomputed from the
traces (X36 47 / 47 and 461 / 461, 0 / 47 on the first form; X38 48 / 48 and 47 / 47; X39 47 / 47; X42 d equal to
X28 d row for row, so the deep-rewind evidence of the entry above carries to chunk137). `exact_from` turns no free
rewind of the server into a replay. The ruling on identical prompts sent together getting different temperature-0
replies in default mode: acceptable. The contract bounds the distribution against exact, not the text; a same-slot
repeat already ends its prompt in an exact window, so sequential identical prompts differed before; the copied
request's last prompt token is the exact one; `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1` or `QX_DS_PREFIX_COPY=0` restores equality.
Kept as a disclosure here and in findings. Its negatives: two branches of the value rule untested and the 6,500
not swept (time only, not exactness); the copy untested beyond 12.8k; `serve1` / `serve3` not run; and, overdue,
the next experiment must attack a row of `perf/presuppositions.md`. Ten text corrections, applied.

## 2026-11-14: The stores' tails lent to the VRAM tier — PROMOTED (set D 19.166 -> 18.640: serve256k3 91.3 -> 83.9, about -7.5% in effect; serve256k1 38.9 -> 34.1-37.0, unscored)

Status: promoted, default on (`QX_DS_STORE_LEND=0` off). A long shot: it attacks presupposition 57
(below), named by the adjudicator's cadence flag.

Belief attacked: a slot's compressed stores must hold VRAM for the whole context from the start, and the expert
tier gets what is left (12.3 of the 20 GiB asked for in a three-slot 256k server; the 8k-context server measured
32.34 / 78.68 ms per token this morning, `w6-serve-013951` on chunk122, against 38.3 / 90.5 at 256k).

Change (`lend3.diff`, sha256 443232e7902c, on 3d05ab2; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk140`: qx-ds-check f39f6aa739ac,
quixiinfer 04c962eefb8b, qx-ds-multi ba58f191c8c7, qx-ds-replay 77bbb9af93f4; `QX_DS_STORE_LEND=0` off,
`QX_DS_STORE_LEND_KEEP` default 32,768 positions):
- At load, for every compressed store and slot, the address range above the kept positions (less the slot's last
  two rows) is recorded, and VRAM slots for the next records of the ranking are carved there, round robin, up to
  the budget asked for: 432 slots, 4.1 GiB, in the three-slot 256k server (476 at a keep of 8,192).
- The driver (`lend_check`) puts a slot's carved addresses on the tier's free lists when the engine lends them and,
  when the engine takes them back, finishes the copies in flight, evicts every record there (table 0 on host and
  device), purges the free and quarantined slots there, synchronizes and acknowledges.
- The engine lends every slot before its first forward, takes a slot's tails back before any forward, chunk,
  unpark or prefix copy that brings it within 1,024 positions of the kept ones (`store_need`), and lends again on
  a reset, a park, or a rewind to under half the kept positions (`store_lend`).
- `flex_check`'s tail test has an upper bound (the arena's end); records in lent ranges are no RAM-dedup sources;
  a record the flex tail evicts without a RAM copy is queued for RAM; `qx-ds-check` takes `QX_DS_CTX`.

Gate (chunk140, `setd-promo35-210457`, preflight 21:04:57: VM shut off, 3,270 MiB used, pressure avg10 0.08, no
build tools, diff 443232e7902c):

| case | baseline | candidate | change |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| decode256k | 38.55 | 38.60 (38.7 / 38.5) | +0.1% |
| prefill256k | 2.00 | 2.00 | 0 |
| serve256k1 | 38.94 | 34.14 | -12.3% |
| serve256k3 | 91.32 | 83.89 | -8.1% |
| decode | 30.70 | 30.90 | +0.7% |
| prefill128 | 10.60 | 10.50 | -0.9% |

Score 19.166 -> 18.640 (-2.7%). Identity serve256k-1 identical; a second serve256k session
`x51-serve256k-2-211223`: 36.62 / 84.46, identical. Tail dumps at 2,048 and 16,384 tokens byte-identical to
chunk137's; ds-checks parts a and b the same text as v11's. The one-slot cases lend nothing (decode256k and
prefill256k unpark at their length; the short ones have the whole budget).

serve256k, every run of the day (ms per token; one repeat each):

| | serve256k1 | serve256k3 |
|---|---|---|
| no lending (13 runs on chunk122-chunk138) | 36.9-39.8, in three modes by the warm-up's arrival order | 90.42-91.58 |
| lending, keep 32,768 (chunk138: X44, X45; chunk140: promo35, X51) | 36.86, 36.46, 34.14, 36.62 | 83.96, 84.32, 83.89, 84.46 |
| lending, keep 8,192 (chunk138) | 36.72 | 83.68 |

serve256k3: -8.1% in the gate run against the committed baseline (the 8% bar cleared by 0.14 points, and by this
run only: the four keep-32,768 sessions read -8.1, -8.1, -7.7, -7.5% against the baseline, the same-binary pairs on
chunk138 -7.2 and -7.4%, and against the day's 13 no-lending runs -6.6 to -8.4%); the two sets are disjoint by 6 ms
(83.68-84.46 against 90.42-91.58). The effect is about 7.5%. A second `--promo` run on chunk140,
`setd-promo36-212326`: 18.712 (decode256k 39.0 / 38.9, serve256k1 36.97, serve256k3 84.10, -7.9%). serve256k1 is not
in the score (the standard cases are decode256k, prefill256k and serve256k3) and is mode-dependent (the breaker:
the two requests of that level trade places by the warm-up's order): lending 34.14-36.97 over five sessions, 34.14
a single fast-mode one; about 36.5 is the reference for later runs.

Scope of the gain: it holds while a slot is under about 31,700 positions; beyond that the slot takes its share back
(134-160 records). The scored serve case uses 40-46-token prompts. With every slot long the numbers are the old
row's. A server at 8,192 positions of context cannot lend at all (it already has the whole budget).

Exactness, lending on against `QX_DS_STORE_LEND=0`:

| test | result |
|---|---|
| X48 t1 (chunk140 `qx-ds-check`, `QX_DS_CTX=262144`, keep 3,072, tiers adapting, exact chunks; 2,560 single steps, rewind to 512, park / unpark to 512) | 2,048 / 2,048 rows bit-identical twice; three take-backs of 160 records each (from decode windows), two re-lends |
| X48 t2 (the same run, the saved state at 2,560 against lending off) | the state files (every store row and ring, 37 MB) identical; 160 records out at the take-back |
| X46 (chunk138, exact server): a short prompt; a 42,065-token prompt reusing that slot beside a decoder (take-back, 138 records); three short prompts at once (lent again); the long prompt afresh (take-back, 134) | 7 / 7 replies and 238 / 238 traced `H` lines equal, in order. On chunk140 (X49): 7 / 7 replies, the per-slot ordered rows identical (741 rows; the two arms interleaved a decoder's steps and a chunk differently) |
| X50 t3 (chunk140, exact server, keep 8,192): a 7,267-token prompt with 260 tokens of reply beside a decoder (take-back, 159 records) | both replies equal, the per-slot ordered rows identical (799 rows; 796 in the first pair, which took nothing back) |
| X50 t4 (chunk140, exact server, keep 8,192): a 12.8k prompt decoding, the same prompt arrives (`copy_prefix` into a lent slot: 157 records out first), then a deep reuse | 3 / 3 replies equal, the per-slot ordered rows identical (640 rows, `P 0 1 12784` among the trace lines) |

Breaker (fresh context): no memory-safety hole found by reading (every store writer indexes by the tokens' own
positions and every path that raises a position is hooked; the highest row a lent slot writes is under the kept
rows for any ratio; reads stop at the slot's position; the take-back's order of copies, holders, lists,
synchronize, acknowledge; the request race). Its findings: the first runs' only exercise of lending was X46 (the
X48 and X50 rows are its tests); serve256k1's modes (the table above); with lending the warm-up's promotions take
the lent slots, the arena tail keeps its initial residents (no RAM copy) and the first chunk sent 304 of them to
NVMe against 26 (now queued for RAM as an eviction does; the first release still returns the tail empty, and it
stays empty through the second chunk: "0 records out, 0 back" twice in X49 and X50 t3, 255 and 310 back later,
against 216 at the first release without lending. No cost seen, in exact-mode servers only: NVMe reads 8,658
against 9,018 in X50 t3 and 21,580 against 22,140 after X49's 42k prompt; 260 tokens after it 12.00 s against
12.47 s, 32 tokens 2.19 against 2.29 s; prompt times equal within 0.1 s);
a take-back evicts the newest promotions (no cost seen: 32 tokens after one 2.21 s against 2.30 s); re-lending only
from half the kept positions (was: just under them) against thrash.

Not covered on chunk140: `copy_prefix` into a lent slot that stays lent (the default keep; X50 t4 forced the
take-back with a keep of 8,192); a re-lend after a deep rewind in the server (the engine path is X48 t1); a
default-mode long prompt with lending (X49 and X50 are exact-mode servers). Not done: the 4.1 GiB is all the stores
can lend; the staging area (256 records, 2.4 GiB) and the reserve (2.5 GiB) are the next idle VRAM. `serve1` /
`serve3` not run (not in `--promo`). X44 and X45's `run.json` carry no diff hash: they ran chunk138 = `lend.diff`
(adbf34420d3f) installed with `--no-build` on a clean tree.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-14/x-store-lend/` (lend.diff, lend2.diff = the first forms, lend3.diff, chain scripts,
log.txt, gate.txt, server-log extracts), `setd-promo35-210457` (promoted; ds-checks attached by hand, as for
promo28), `setd-promo36-212326`, `x51-serve256k-2-211223`, `x44-*`, `x45-*`.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-14): PROMOTE, default on
Gate and score reproduced (19.166 -> 18.640; serve256k1 is not scored, so its modes cannot change the score). The
8% bar is cleared by the gate run only, by 0.14 points; accepted under the rule as written (the promotion run's
median against the committed baseline, outside the spread on both sides); the scoreboard row says the effect is
about 7.5%. Exactness recomputed from the traces: per-slot ordered rows and every chunk hash identical between
lending on and off in all five pairs, the state files of X48 t2 byte-identical. Memory safety as far as reading
goes: one size class per record size; a lent slot stays under keep - 1,024 positions, its highest row under
keep / ratio + 2; the take-back's order; the flex tail's upper bound; inert without the driver (that every store
writer is hooked and that reads stop at the position are the breaker's claims). The side effect on the flex tail is
adequately measured for a landing, in exact mode only. Presupposition 57 overturned: the cadence flag is cleared.
Nine text corrections, applied.


## 2026-11-15: Tail residents give up their RAM copies — PROMOTED (set D 18.640 -> 18.475: serve256k3 83.9 -> 80.1-81.6 over five sessions, -2.8 to -4.5%, about -3.6%; found by an inventory that refuted "more idle VRAM")

Status: promoted (`setd-promo39-002914`, code ff2326d). One driver change, two knobs (`QX_DS_FLEX_DEDUP`, `QX_DS_LEND_DEDUP`, both default
on, `=0` restores the old behaviour of each).

**The question was the backlog's item 1** ("more idle VRAM in a short-context server: the staging area, the
reserve; the 8k server's 32.3 / 78.7 is what a full 20 GiB tier gives"). Its premise was false, in two steps:

1. *The lent tails are all the stores have.* X53 (a carve that lets smaller records fill a range's end) changed
   432 slots to 434: the 12 ranges hold 4.10 GiB and 0.04 GiB of it is not carved. X54 / X55 (`QX_DS_MEMLOG`
   checkpoints through the load, diff kept in `x-vram-inventory/`, not landed): of 31.41 GiB the three-slot 256k
   server has 30.83 free at the start; scratch 0.56; layers, rings and stores 12.13 (stores 4.69: three 2:1 layers
   at 0.94 and one 1:1 layer at 1.88; dense weights and rings 7.25, derived: no line prints it); output 0.51; staging and prefetch areas 2.88; arena 12.39;
   2.15 free after load, 1.21 after the warm-up.
2. *The 8k server never had a 20 GiB tier*: its log says 16.9 GiB (1,770 records). With the tails lent the 256k
   server has 16.4. X56 (same session, chunk140): 8k 34.0, 35.0 / 79.24, 79.24; 256k 36.55 / 84.67. (X56's 256k
   server log was overwritten by a second, cut-off session.) The gap at near-equal tiers was NVMe reads: 28.2-28.7k
   per session (X57's two sessions) against 21.0k, VRAM hits equal within 1%.

**Cause.** `QX_DS_CHUNK=0` on the 256k server (no flex tail: X58 a-1) gives 32.89 / 80.26 and 20,974 NVMe reads, the
8k server's figures. The driver frees the RAM copy of a record that is resident in VRAM ("dedup": the place goes
to a record that would otherwise stay on NVMe), except for residents of the flex tail (their copy fed the refill
after a chunk) and, since efb14f0, of the lent store tails. The flex test had no upper bound, so it also covered the
lent slots (inferred: X57 and rows d-1 / d-2 below; no log prints the addresses): up to 318 + 432 slots of
residents kept two copies, and as many records sat on NVMe instead of RAM.

**Change.** Both exclusions are off by default; the flex test got its upper bound so that the knobs are
independent. Nothing else: a chunk taking the tail (`flex_check`) and a slot taking its tails back (`lend_check`)
already queue an evicted record without a RAM copy for RAM, and the refill already skips a record without one.

**Measurement** (`bench.sh --cases serve256k`, one binary per row pair, knobs in the environment, VM off):

| arm | serve256k1 | serve256k3 | NVMe reads | dedups |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| both off (chunk143 b-2, c-1; chunk140's behaviour, by the code, not by binary) | 35.80, 38.61 | 83.82, 83.75 | 27.7k, 28.2k | 5.7k |
| both on (chunk143 b-1, c-2) | 33.11, 35.35 | 81.57, 81.23 | 22.8k, 23.2k | 10.2k, 10.3k |
| flex on, lend off (chunk144 d-1) | 35.31 | 82.44 | 25.5k | 7.6k |
| flex off, lend on (chunk144 d-2) | 35.96 | 82.24 | 24.1k | 8.2k |
| no flex tail (`QX_DS_CHUNK=0`, a-1, on chunk142) | 32.89 | 80.26 | 21.0k | 9.9k |

## 2026-11-15: The start-up burst of stage fallbacks — LOCATED (the server's warm-up, before any request; no change)

Question (backlog item 1 of 2026-11-15, the adjudicator's open item): where do the ~105 fallbacks of a session with
`QX_DS_LEND_DEDUP` on come from. Diagnostic (`QX_DS_FALLBACK_LOG`, not landed:
`perf/results/2026-11-15/x-fallback-burst/fallback-log-diagnostic.diff`) printing every fallback with the host and
device table words, one short three-client session each (X67: 104, X68: 101):
- every one is at driver tick 1, layer 39, experts 27 and 14, in jobs of 2 tokens with 4 staged selections;
- the record is in VRAM in a lent slot and not in RAM, on the host and on the device (table word non-zero, RAM
  table word zero), not in flight, not queued anywhere;
- all of them precede the line "window graph (1 tokens) captured": they are in the warm-up, and no request's step
  has one.
About 34 identical jobs for one layer in one tick, listing a record the device table already holds, are consistent
with the warm-up's dry and capture passes being served from a mailbox that still carries the previous real job's
staged list (inferred: not confirmed by a trace of the mailbox sequence). The first tick's promotions fill the
freshly lent slots and dedup frees their RAM copies, so the stale list names records the pool must now compute;
before the dedup change the copies stayed and the same stale jobs were staged for nothing. No cost to a request and
none to exactness (X62's rows cover the warm-up). Closed as an open item; what is left is hygiene: a dry pass
should not be served as a job at all.

## 2026-11-15: A smaller staging area, the chunk jobs' ring in the flex tail — REJECTED as a default (decode -3 to -4% at three clients; prompts of under 64 tokens +30%, decode after a long prompt slower)

Question (backlog item 2): the staging area (256 records, 2.88 GiB with the prefetch slots) is the largest idle VRAM
left; a decode step stages a handful. Hypothesis: a quarter of it serves decode, and prompt chunks can borrow their
ring with the flex tail.

**Knob only** (`QX_DS_STAGE_CAP`, chunk144, set D `--promo`, one run each; baseline 18.475):

| records | tier (3-slot 256k) | decode256k | prefill256k | serve256k1 | serve256k3 | decode | prefill128 | score |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 256 (baseline) | 12.3 GiB | 39.00 | 2.00 | 32.98 | 80.84 | 31.0 | 10.5 | 18.475 |
| 128 | 13.7 | 38.40 | 2.0 | 34.40 | 79.30 | 30.2 | 13.2 | 18.262 |
| 64 | 14.4 | 38.40 | 2.0 | 32.88 | 77.58 | 29.8 | 16.6 | 18.129 |

prefill128 (windows of 128, no chunks) loses 26% and 58%. Long prompts in the server (X63's scenario, three ~13k
prompts at once): the three full-stack chunks 5,892 / 5,583 / 5,389 ms at 256, 6,181 / 6,149 / 5,894 at 64 (+8%),
9,455 / 9,512 / 9,159 at 32; KV-only chunks unchanged at 64, +43% at 32.

**The chunk ring in the flex tail** (`ring1.diff`, `ring2.diff` with the breaker's fixes; binaries chunk145,
chunk146; not landed): chunk jobs stage through a ring of `QX_DS_CHUNK_RING` records (256) at the end of the flex
tail, held with the chunk buffers; the staging area defaults to 64 when that ring exists. The release waits for
the chunk jobs and synchronizes the stage streams (under the capture lock) before the slots carry records again.

| | serve256k3 | serve256k1 | decode256k | prefill128 | score |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| chunk145, `x69-ring` | 78.25 | 33.12 | 38.80 | 10.5 | 18.244 |
| chunk145, `setd-promo40` (diff in the tree; a 7 s cargo build may have overlapped its end: my slip) | 78.31 | 33.03 | 38.95 | 10.6 | 18.272 |

Exact: 7 / 7 replies of X59's scenario equal to chunk143's (X71); X62's take-back scenarios' rows 540 / 540, 261 /
261, 546 / 546, 96 / 96 equal to chunk144's (X72); two rounds of three merged short prompts against
`QX_DS_CHUNK_RING=0 QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=256` on chunk146: 6 / 6 replies equal and the 1,590 traced rows equal as a
multiset (the requests landed on different slots, so not per slot; X75 rows); the breaker's capture-race case
(`qx-ds-check`, no warm-up, `QX_DS_CHUNK_SKIP=0`) ran clean (X75 a1). ds-checks do not exercise the ring (no
`QX_DS_CHUNK`): same text as before.

**What it costs** (the breaker's B2 and C1, measured):

| X63's scenario (chunk146 unless said) | full-stack chunks, ms | 52 post-prompt steps, ms | NVMe reads |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| staging 256, no ring (chunk144, `x63-ref2`) | 5,892 / 5,583 / 5,389 | 5,010 | 28.3k |
| staging 64, no ring (chunk144, `x63-cap64`) | 6,181 / 6,149 / 5,894 | 4,920 | 24.5k |
| staging 64, ring 256 in the tail | 5,873 / 5,616 / 5,393 | 5,385 | 29.5k |
| staging 64, ring 128 in the tail | 6,075 / 5,924 / 5,702 | 5,183 | 28.2k |

The ring restores the chunk times and makes the tail 622 slots instead of 318 (40% of the tier out at every
prompt, none with a RAM copy since the dedup change): the steps after a prompt lose 0.4 s over three prompts.

| three clients, prompts of 49-60 tokens, 16 tokens each, five rounds (X75 short2) | prompt seconds, summed | generation seconds, summed | staged / pool selections |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| staging 64, ring in the tail (the candidate default) | 47.5 | 23.9 | 43k / 45k |
| staging 256, ring in the tail | 36.2 | 25.6 | 84k / 15k |
| staging 256, no ring (the landed behaviour) | 37.3 | 25.2 | 85k / 11k |

Three such prompts run as windows of 48 and 96, which overflow a 64-record area into the pool: +30% of prompt time
for the commonest chat turn (a short message on a reused prefix), against -5 to -7% of generation time.

**Verdict: REJECTED as a default.** The gain (serve256k3 -3.2%, about -4% with the knob alone) is under the 8% a new
buffer class needs and the knob alone regresses a standard case (prefill128) and short prompts. Not a promotion,
nothing landed. For a decode-only deployment `QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=64` is available today (three clients -4%; prompts
under 64 tokens +30%, long prompts' full-stack chunks +8%). What would make it landable: the staging area's upper
part lent to the tier and taken back before a window wider than a few tokens (the store tails' mechanism), so that
every prompt path keeps 256 records; expected at most the -4% above.

Also settled here: the reserve (2.56 GiB, 1.21 free after the warm-up) could give about 0.5 GiB, under 1% by the
4.1 GiB = 7.5% scale: not pursued. decode256k read 38.3-38.5 with the larger tier and 38.7-39.1 in every run at the
baseline's tier: the baseline's 39.00 is its level, promo35's 38.60 was a low draw.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-15/x-chunk-ring/` (ring1.diff, ring2.diff, chain scripts, log.txt, server-log extracts),

## 2026-11-15: A structured-output constraint waits for the reasoning to end — LANDED (a functional fix: `json_object` / `json_schema` / `guided_*` replies with thinking on came back in `reasoning_content` with an empty `content`)

Not a performance change; the engine is untouched (qx-ds-check 86b73a06c964 as chunk144's). Server layer only
(`crates/qx-serve`: worker.rs, main.rs, worker_ds.rs; `x-json-think/json-think.diff`; binaries chunk148,
quixiinfer 2d64a25e2271).

Cause: the chat template opens a reasoning block; the grammar mask applied from the first generated token, so
`</think>` could never be sampled and the splitter filed the whole constrained reply as reasoning (X33, the
backlog's oldest functional bug). Fix: (1) a request whose rendered prompt ends with an open `<think>` starts with
the constraint held off; no mask and no matcher step until the generated text contains `</think>`, then the
constraint applies from the next token (what vLLM's reasoning-aware structured output does); (2) "the reply opens
in a reasoning block" is now read from the rendered prompt, not from the request's flag, for the constraint and for
the reply's split alike: an assistant prefill (the block closed in the prompt) used to have its whole
continuation filed as reasoning, constrained or not.

| request (temperature 0; X77) | chunk144 | chunk148 |
|---|---|---|
| `guided_regex [A-Z]{3}-[0-9]{4}`, thinking on | content empty, 8 chars in reasoning | content `CAP-0001`, reasoning 451 chars, 103 tokens |
| `json_schema` with keys the prompt does not name, thinking on | content empty, 35 chars in reasoning, 17 tokens (JSON per X76) | content `{ "stadt": "Paris", "einwohner": 68.4 }`, reasoning 451 chars, 119 tokens |
| the same, thinking off | valid, 16 tokens | identical |
| the regex after an assistant prefill (`wo_eos`) | content empty | content `IES-2024` |
| a prefill, no constraint | content empty, 106 chars in reasoning | the 106 chars in content |
| no constraint, thinking on | 129 chars content, 451 reasoning | identical |
| `json_object`, `max_tokens` 50, thinking on | 14 tokens, 60 chars in reasoning (JSON per X76) | finish `length`, content empty, 241 chars of reasoning |

The last row is the behaviour change to know: a constrained request now spends its reasoning first (about 100-150
tokens here, 140 in X76), so a small `max_tokens` can end inside it. More generally, a constrained reply that ends
inside the reasoning for any reason (`max_tokens`, EOS, a `stop` string, the context) has an empty `content`: the
constraint is only guaranteed once `</think>` is generated. `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` gives the
short reply in `content` (16 tokens in X77; 14 for `json_object` in X76 on chunk147). X77 is non-streaming; the
streaming path shares the splitter's flag (by reading, not exercised). X76 (the first form: flag from the request) gave valid JSON in content for
`json_object` and `json_schema`, but could not tell the constraint from the model's own answer; X77 can.

Breaker (fresh context, read-only): found the first form's hole (the flag set from `chat && thinking`: with a
prefill or a lone system message the block is not open, `</think>` never comes and the constraint would never
apply) and that X76 did not discriminate: both fixed above. Checked clean: the `emit` refactor for
unconstrained requests; `</think>` is one vocabulary entry and decodes at once; JSON accepts the leading newlines;
the splitter and the worker flip at the same point; the completions endpoint and the CLI are unchanged; no other
consumer of the matcher. Left as it was: `tools` together with a constraint (the mask forbids a tool-call block
after the reasoning; the server parses no tool calls either way); the template's `response_format` section is not
fed (the model reasons without having seen the schema); the other worker (`worker.rs`, not run here) gets the
field and keeps its behaviour.

Server identity and timing: chunk147 (the first form, X58 e) "identical", 34.61 / 81.17; chunk148 (X58 f)
"identical", 33.01 / 81.23.

Left open by the final form (the adjudicator): after `task: action` the template opens `<think>` before the task
token, and after a trailing `latest_reminder` there is no generation prompt: the prompt does not end with
`<think>`, so the server now treats such a reply as content from the first token and applies a constraint at once
(before: split at `</think>`). Untested; the comment in `main.rs` names "a task token" among the closed cases, which
is right for the other task tokens only. The better test is "the last `<think>` in the prompt has no `</think>`
after it" (backlog).

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-15): LANDED
The diff does what the entry says and touches no engine code (qx-ds-check, qx-ds-multi and qx-ds-replay hash as
chunk144's; the binary of X58 f is the diff's). Every table cell matches the two outputs and the servers' request
lines. The unconstrained path is unchanged for an ordinary chat (the prompt ends with an open block, so the new flag
equals the old; "plain think" is 129 / 451 chars and 131 tokens on both binaries). Two identity sessions, none
mismatching. Seven text corrections, applied.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-15/x-json-think/`.

## 2026-11-15: Strata head-to-head, refreshed (measurement only): ahead on every prompt length and on decode

Question: where do we stand against the goal ("match or exceed Strata's prefill and decode") before choosing the
next experiment. Same box, same model files, the same ids (route16k prefixes), VM off, one run each (X81, X82).
Strata: `strata-dsv41 --max-context 32768 --experts 6 --ssd-cache 95 --prefill-chunk 8192 --dspark none --n 64`, a
fresh process per run. Ours: chunk150 `qx-ds-check`, `QX_DS_CHUNK=8192 QX_DS_WARM=1 QX_DS_TAIL=64`, default (KL-bounded)
prompt chunks, a fresh process per run (kernels warmed, tiers as loaded); the prompt's last 64 tokens run as single
steps, so the chunk covers N - 64 rows.

| prompt tokens | Strata, prompt | ours, the chunk(s) of N - 64 rows | Strata, 64 tokens after | ours, 64 single steps after |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 128 | 4,019 ms | 1,097 ms (64 rows) | 18.1 tok/s | 26.3 |
| 256 | 4,806 | 1,911-1,921 (192) | 18.9 | 26.8-27.3 |
| 1,024 | 7,261 | 2,344 (960) | 16.7 | 25.3 |
| 2,048 | 10,446 | 3,113 (1,984) | 17.5 | 25.9 |
| 16,384 | 19,185 (854 tok/s) | 4,791 + 6,934 (16,320 rows: 1,392 tok/s) | 16.5 | 26.9 |

Not like for like in two ways, neither large enough to change the order: Strata's prompt time is for all N tokens
and ours for N - 64 (at 38 ms a step the 64 would add 2.4 s as single steps, far less inside the chunk); Strata's
process is cold where ours has compiled its kernels (the 16k comparison of 2026-11-11 on fresh processes read
841-880 against 1,132-1,158 tok/s). Strata has no server: nothing to compare the three-client case with.
**The goal's two lines are met**: prefill 1.6x (16k) to 3.4x (2k) faster, decode about 1.5x. What is left is our
own ceiling (the backlog's queue of 2026-11-15, evening).

Found on the way: `qx-ds-check` with `QX_DS_WARM=1` and a prompt of 128 tokens dies with CUDA error 700 (the tool
sizes the context to the prompt, 256 here, and the warm-up's windows run to position 446); with `QX_DS_CTX=8192` it
runs. A tool guard, not a server path (the server's context is 8,192 at least): backlog.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-15/x-strata-h2h/`.

## 2026-11-15: Three-client decode, the two knob levers re-swept (measurement only): both flat, the defaults stand

After the tier grew from 12.3 to 16.4 GiB and the tail residents' duplicates went, the two settings tuned on the
old tier were swept again on chunk150 (`bench.sh --cases serve256k`, one session each, VM off, identity
"identical" in all six):

| setting | serve256k1 | serve256k3 | staged / pool selections |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| default (`QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE_NARROW=4`, `QX_DS_PROMOS=32`; X58 e, f, g) | 34.6, 33.0, 34.7, 35.7 | 81.2, 81.2, 80.7, 80.3 | 261k / 184k |
| narrow share 2 | 34.5 | 86.1 | 184k / 264k |
| narrow share 3 | 35.5 | 82.3 | 224k / 225k |
| narrow share 5 | 35.6 | 81.6 | 301k / 146k |
| promotions 16 per token | 37.0 | 80.1 | 264k / 188k |
| promotions 64 | 33.0 | 80.3 | 251k / 194k |
| promotions 96 | 34.6 | 80.6 | 261k / 184k |

VRAM hits are 60.6-60.8% of the selections in every arm (39% miss with 11% of the records resident); the split of
the misses has its optimum where it was, and the promotion budget does not move the hit rate. With the findings'
ceilings (the pool at the DRAM's read bandwidth, a short prompt at the disk's) the three-client step is bound by
the bytes of the experts outside VRAM, not by a setting. A short chunk shows the same: 64 rows touch 99 distinct
experts per layer (1,097 ms), 192 rows 150 (1,921 ms): about 1 GB of records per layer whatever the row count.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-15/x58-h-*`, `x58-i-*`.

## 2026-11-16: The rings checkpointed at a prompt's end — LANDED, default on, not a promotion (a chat turn after a reply of 128+ tokens reuses its history: 2.7k tokens 3.7 -> 0.4-2.1 s of prompt time; a rewind after a rewind read stale ring cells, fixed)

Presupposition attacked (61): "a chat's next turn reuses its history". It did only when the client sent the reply
back whole. A client that drops the reasoning from the history (the usual one) shares the previous prompt and
nothing after it; the slot stands at the reply's end, and once the reply is longer than the rings' reach (DS_TMAX =
128 positions) the rewind to the prompt's end is a deep one: under 8,192 tokens the scheduler takes the prompt
afresh (and an empty slot), above it a 4.4 s replay.

Measured on HEAD (chunk150, X83 head; three slots at 256k; a three-turn chat over a 2.5k-token text, 400 tokens
per reply at most, the history resent without the reasoning; one run):

| turn | thinking on: prompt tokens (reused), prompt s | thinking off |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2,500 (0), 4.07 | 1,870 (0), 3.08 |
| 2 | 2,688 (0), 3.68 | 2,022 (2,004), 0.47 |
| 3 | 2,700 (0), 3.74 | 2,234 (2,220), 0.38 |

Change (`ckpt2.diff`, sha256 79b38d5a8a1c, on 7c70dfb; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk153`: qx-ds-check 94a7df6a62e1,
quixiinfer df915b573d5b; chunk152 = the first form, `ckpt1.diff`, before the breaker's two points):
1. `DsEngine::checkpoint(slot)`: the slot's rings (per layer `ring_w * head_dim` floats) downloaded to the host with
   the position and `exact_from`; one per slot, the latest. The server calls it when a prompt of 256+ tokens ends
   (the first sampled token). `QX_DS_RING_CKPT=0`: none. Trace op `S slot` (`qx-ds-replay` replays it).
2. `rewind(slot, p)`: when the rings no longer reach `p` and the checkpoint does (`p` at most 128 positions below
   what the checkpointed rings were written up to, and at or above its `exact_from`), the rings are uploaded and
   the slot is at `p`: no replay. `rewind_fast` says so, which is what the scheduler's value rule asks.
3. The checkpoint is dropped whenever the slot's tokens below its position change (reset, a rewind below it, a
   replay below it, park, unpark); `copy_prefix` hands the source's checkpoint to the destination (the same tokens),
   so a cross-slot copy of a prefix the scheduler valued through the source's checkpoint is free too.
4. **A defect of the old rewind, fixed** (breaker, by reading; shown by X87): a ring cell holds the last position
   written with its index, so after a rewind from H the cells the slot has not reached again hold the old
   sequence's positions up to H and the positions `ring_w` below them are gone. `rewind` tested `pos - p <= 128`
   alone. `ring_hi` per slot (the highest position written since the rings were last whole) is now in the test
   (`max(pos, ring_hi) - p <= 128`), in the checkpoint, and `snapshot` replays first when the rings have such
   cells. `QX_DS_RING_HI=0` is the old test, kept only to show the defect.
5. `qx-ds-check`: `QX_DS_CKPT=C` (a checkpoint at position C of the straight pass), `QX_DS_REWIND_VIA=P1:K` (a
   rewind to P1 and K tokens before the rewind under test), "(free)" / "(replay)" in the line when either is set.
   `tools/ds-checks.sh` part c: lines 8 (two) and 9; `check_ds_log.py` judges them when present.

The defect, shown (X87, chunk153 with `QX_DS_RING_HI=0`, exact kernels): 600 single steps, rewind to 500, 20
tokens, rewind to 400 (taken as free): **0 / 200 rows bit-identical**, KL mean 0.124, max 2.04, top-1 176 / 200.
With the fix the same case replays: 200 / 200 (line 9). In the server it needed a prompt that shares up to 128
positions less than the slot holds, ends below the slot's old end, and a third prompt sharing less again while the
slot is still below that end (an edited last message, twice): rare, silent, and on HEAD since the ring rewind
(2026-11-06). The checkpoint's first form would have frozen such rings and served them however long the reply.

Exactness (exact kernels, `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`):

| test | result |
|---|---|
| ds-checks parts a, b, c on chunk153 (`ds-checks-chunk153.log`) | pass; lines 1-7 the same text as chunk150's (X79) |
| line 8: checkpoint at 420 of 560, rewind to 400 | free, 160 / 160 rows bit-identical |
| line 8: checkpoint at 420 of 600, rewind to 400 (the checkpoint), 10 tokens, rewind to 300 (the rings, `ring_hi` 420) | free, 300 / 300 |
| line 9: rewind to 400 after a rewind to 500 and 20 tokens, of 600 | replay, 200 / 200 |
| X83 ckx against frx (chunk152; `QX_DS_PREFIX=0` = every prompt afresh): the three-turn chat, thinking on and off | six replies byte-identical (1,746 / 1,690 / 560 and 651 / 692 / 259 bytes); only the thinking-on turns 2 and 3 restore from a checkpoint (2 positions below it, `exact_from` 0), the thinking-off turns are ring rewinds as on HEAD; on chunk152, not the final binary |
| X88 ck against fr (chunk153): a 5.2k-token turn (a chunk with KV-only rows: `exact_from` > 0), the same text with another question (the restore 60 positions below the checkpoint), two more turns (the third after a prompt that ended in a window), a second client decoding 700 tokens beside (it ended during turn 4's reply, after every restore) | four replies and the side reply byte-identical (976 / 914 / 825 / 927 / 3,589 bytes); prompt seconds 11.44, 0.50, 0.37, 0.92 against 11.4 each |
| X89 (chunk153): the same 2.5k prompt again 12 s and 18 s after the first (the source ~93 and ~259 tokens into its reply: the first is inside the rings' reach, the second is copied with the source's checkpoint) | 2,498 reused in 0.11 / 0.09 s (7.44 s with `QX_DS_PREFIX_COPY=0`); both replies equal each other and the no-copy arm's (1,746 bytes) |

The server tests compare temperature-0 texts, not rows (200-700 tokens each); the rows are the check tool's lines.
Not run: a trace replayed with `QX_DS_RING_CKPT=0`; a checkpoint taken after a deep replay (8,192+ shared); a
row-level check of a checkpoint taken after a chunk or a window, and of `copy_prefix` with a checkpoint (texts
only); `snapshot`'s replay branch (not reachable from the server); a restore at exactly the checkpoint's position;
a KL figure for default mode (the claim rests on the exact-mode equivalence).

Timing, default mode (X83, one run each; chunk150 against chunk153; turn 1 is 4.07 s in both):

| turn (thinking on) | chunk150 prompt s | chunk153 prompt s | client s |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| 2: 2,688 tokens, 2,498 reused | 3.68 | 2.12 (a 190-row chunk) | 16.97 -> 15.81 |
| 3: 2,700 tokens, 2,686 reused | 3.74 | 0.41 (14 tokens in a window) | 10.76 -> 5.81 (the reply is 153 tokens against 208: other numerics, as any reuse in default mode) |

What is left of turn 2 is the short chunk's floor (backlog: 64-255 tokens ~1.8 s). The reply after a reused prompt
decoded 0.4-0.6 s slower over 400 tokens in three pairs (13.69 and 13.90 against 13.28 s; exact mode 13.66 against
13.28: 3-5%): a fresh chunk leaves the conversation's experts in VRAM, a reuse does not (turn 2's reply has +2,002
RAM evaluations and +439 ms of pool time on chunk153 against chunk150, +2,366 and +299 ms in ckx against frx). The
checkpoint itself (a sync and 40 downloads) has no timing print; the breaker read 3.4-8.4 ms from the gap before
the first post-prompt step in `x83-ck.server.log` against 0.1 ms on HEAD.

Gate: set D `setd-promo43-000649` (chunk153, the diff in the tree) 18.395 against 18.475 (-0.4%): decode256k 38.9 /
38.9, prefill256k 2.0, serve256k1 35.68, serve256k3 80.00, decode 31.0, prefill128 10.5; identity serve256k-1
identical. Preflight 00:06:49: VM shut off, 3,445 MiB used, pressure avg10 0.00, no build tools. `perf_diff.py
gate` flags serve256k1 (+8.2% against the baseline's 32.98): the case wanders run to run (the eight default-settings sessions
since the dedup default: 32.98-37.66, the baseline's the lowest). `run.json` records correctness as skipped: the
pass is `check_ds_log.py` on the three part logs of 94a7df6a62e1. The gate's prompts are 42-46 tokens: it takes no checkpoint and restores none, so it
shows no harm, not the gain. A first gate on chunk152 was stopped when the breaker's report came.

Breaker (fresh context): the `ring_hi` hole (point 4) and the cross-slot valuation (point 3: a running slot's
prefix was valued as free through a checkpoint `copy_prefix` could not use, which would have stalled every decoder
for a replay) found by reading; its tests are lines 8-9, X87, X88 and X89. Checked and found sound: every writer
that lowers a position drops the checkpoint; the server never parks; `upload_at` is synchronous; lent store tails
lie above any live checkpoint.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-16/x-ring-ckpt/`, `setd-promo43-000649`.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-16): HOLD, then LANDED, default on, not a promotion
The hold: one identity session on chunk153, the tree uncommitted, nine text corrections (applied above). Lifted by
two more `serve256k` sessions on chunk153 (X58 j: `QX_DS_RING_CKPT=1` 35.51 / 80.91, `=0` 34.75 / 81.04, both
"identical") and the structured-output requests (X78 c153: all eight as on chunk148, 0 panics). The win is on the
secondary metric (a chat turn's prompt time); set D shows no harm. Also measured (X91): the card's PCIe link is
Gen5 x16 throughout a 13k-token prompt and reply (it idles at Gen1).

## 2026-11-16: decode256k per kernel (measurement only): 38% waiting for host-served experts, 20% dense GEMVs at ~1.1 TB/s, 14% position cost

`nsys profile -t cuda` of the gate's decode256k command on chunk153 (X93; 39.2 ms per token under the profiler,
38.9 without). The graph replays hide their kernels: the kernel sums cover the two uncaptured steps (80 launches
of the per-layer kernels), 68.6 ms in all, so per step:

| kernel | ms per step | launches | note |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| `qx_moe_wait_staged` + `qx_moe_wait` | 9.7 + 5.0 | 40 + 40 | the card waiting for experts outside VRAM (2026-11-07: 17.0 + 10.8) |
| `qx_mgemv` | 6.9 | 527 (13.0 us each) | the dense f32 GEMVs: ~7.25 GiB read per token, ~1.1 TB/s |
| `qx_ds_idx_score2` | 2.25 | 8 (283 us) | position cost |
| `qx_ds_attn_part` + `attn_merge` | 1.5 + 0.5 | 40 + 40 | position cost in part |
| `qx_rms_groups` | 1.25 | 241 | |
| `qx_ds_topk2` | 1.15 | 9 (127 us) | position cost (2.5 ms before the tiled kernel) |
| `qx_ds_copy_f32` | 0.7 | 98 | |
| expert kernels (gate-up, down, post, combine) | ~2.5 | | |
| the rest | ~3 | | |

The position cost is ~5 ms of the 8 ms between decode256k (38.9) and decode (31.0); the rest is the tier (15.4
against 17.1 GiB, and 32 tokens of adaptation after the state's load against 1,000). No single kernel holds 3% of
the step except the waits (host-bound: findings, the DRAM's read bandwidth), `qx_mgemv` (20%, already within
~1.6x of the card's nominal memory bandwidth; exact f32 by contract) and `qx_ds_idx_score2` (5.8%).

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-16/x-nsys-dec256k/`.

## 2026-11-16: A short chunk's experts on the grouped kernels — LANDED, default on, not a promotion (chunks under 256 rows -12 to -15% for a first chunk, to -20% on later 64-row chunks: 192 rows 1,920 -> 1,650 ms; a chat turn's 190-token message 2.12 -> 1.72 s)

Presupposition attacked (62): "a short prompt's time is its experts' bytes: the copy link and the disk" (findings;
the notebook's entry of 2026-11-15). Measured instead:

1. **The link is not the limit.** `qx-membw`, extended for the run (not landed, its output not retained: the
   figures are from the session): the copy engine alone moves
   50.9-51.3 GB/s host to device, the same on 1 to 16 streams and for 9.5 MB or 67 MB copies; beside the pool
   28.3 (the pool 29.3, together 57.6: the DRAM's read ceiling).
2. **The pool does not help a window** (X94, prefill128 on chunk153, `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE` 8 / 7 / 6 / 5): 10.7 /
   10.7 / 11.4 / 13.0 ms per token. Staging everything stays the default.
3. **A 192-row chunk is GPU-bound on its expert kernels** (X95, nsys on chunk153; the window from the chunk's
   first large host-to-device copy to its last, 2,012 ms; the chunk line says 1,984 ms):
   5,566 copies, 57.1 GB, the copy engine busy 1,209 ms (47.3 GB/s while copying, idle ~21 ms a layer); kernels
   busy 1,584 ms, 1,225 of it expert kernels (6,009 experts: `qx_moe_gateup_mma_*` 102-148 us each, `down_mma`
   39-67 us, whatever the rows: 7.7 rows an expert on average, ~204 us an expert in all); the card idle 50-70 ms.
   6,009 - 5,566 = 443 of the used experts were not copied: the VRAM residents (a derivation, not a counter).
4. The chunk's expert kernels are the exact int8 tensor-core ones (64 rows a pass); the grouped CUDA-core family
   (a weight block decoded once per warp for up to 8 rows; `QX_DS_CHUNK_MMA=0`) and the per-selection family
   (`QX_DS_CHUNK_GROUPED=0`) are the same bits. On short chunks (X96, X97; chunk153):

| rows | tensor-core (default) | grouped | per selection |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 192 | 1,926 / 1,918 (exact mode: the same kernels) | 1,646 | 1,719 |
| 64 | 1,095 / 1,097 | 964 | 984 |

Change (`grp3.diff`, sha256 1680be89c1d7, on 7943d9b; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk156`: qx-ds-check 772a7b95eae8,
quixiinfer 059163839b16): in a chunk whose experts run one after another on the main stream (`ns == 1`: under 256
rows by default) an expert with at most `QX_DS_CHUNK_GRP_MAX` rows (default 64; 0: never) runs the grouped
kernels, one with more the tensor-core ones. Both families were already warmed at start-up.

Threshold and the forms tried (one run a cell; 192 rows repeated: tensor-core 1,918 / 1,920 / 1,926 / 1,928 over
three binaries, grouped 1,645 / 1,646 / 1,652 / 1,654 / 1,668; the 136-row row is X100 on chunk156 with the logits
dump on, its "8" cell is threshold 4; chunk154 = the knob with default 0, chunk155 = also a
`QX_DS_CHUNK_SIDE_MIN` knob and the grouped choice on the side streams, not landed):

| rows | 0 (as before) | 8 | 24 | 64 | side streams from 1 row, 0 / 24 / 64 | grouped on the side streams, 24 / 64 |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 64 | 1,097 | | 963 | | 976 / 971 / - | |
| 136 | 1,596 | 1,394 (4) | | 1,389 | | |
| 192 | 1,920 | 1,668 | 1,652 | 1,654 | 1,665 / 1,665 / 1,669 | |
| 448 (side streams) | 1,790 | | 1,789 | | | 1,808 / 1,816 |
| 1,024 (side streams) | 2,404 | 2,377 | 2,379 | 2,374 | | 2,427 / 2,551 |
| 4,096 | 4,337 | | 4,339 | 4,328 | | |

Overlapping the experts on side streams and the grouped kernels are substitutes under 256 rows (both land on
~1,650 ms at 192 rows: the copies' 1.2 s and the dense layers are what is left); the grouped kernels on the side
streams cost the larger chunks, so the side branch is untouched. With `QX_DS_CHUNK_STREAMS=1` (not the default)
the new branch also serves large chunks: 1,024 rows 4,007 -> 3,317 ms (X103; timed, not hashed; the same holds
whenever `ns` falls to 1: a staging ring under 8 x streams, raw launches off). X103's sixteen 64-row chunks: -13.4
to -20.0% each against the knob at 0, mean -17.0%.

Exactness (all rows compared by the sha256 of the logits dump; chunk156):

| test | result |
|---|---|
| X100: the 64 single steps after a 192-row chunk and after a 136-row chunk: default (64), tensor-core only (0), both families in one chunk (4), no chunk at all (single steps from 0) | the same hash in the four arms, both sizes |
| X103: 1,088 tokens in sixteen 64-row chunks, every row dumped (each chunk's own last row included: the top layers' experts), 64 / 0 / 4 | the same hash (562,626,560 bytes) |
| X103: 400 single steps, rewind to 200 through a 200-row replay chunk (exact kernels), 64 and 0 | 200 / 200 rows bit-identical in both |
| ds-checks parts a, b, c (`ds-checks-chunk156.log`) | pass; the same text as chunk153's (its chunks are 400+ rows: the untouched branch) |

Several slots in one short chunk (X103, the server, two requests of ~100 tokens together, twice): with the knob
at 0 requests 1 and 2 shared a chunk (201 rows of 2 slots, 2,237 ms, tensor-core); at 64 the four went into
separate chunks (grouped). The four replies are byte-identical between the arms. Not run: a several-slot chunk on
the grouped kernels (the kernels index by selection; the breaker expects no difference); formats other than this
model's; the X103 rewind arms do not show that the replay chunk took the grouped branch (bit-identical either way).

Server (X83 ck156 against ck153, the three-turn chat, one run): turn 2's 190-row chunk 2,117 -> 1,720 ms (prompt
2.12 -> 1.72 s, -18.8%); the other prompts as before (4.07, 0.41, 3.10, 0.45, 0.37 s); the six reply texts are
byte-identical to ck153's. Four first prompts of 87-101 tokens (X103): chunks of 1.16-1.38 s (prompt times 1.38 /
1.91 / 1.25 / 1.60 s: two waited behind the other's chunk).

Gate: set D `setd-promo44-085725` (chunk156, the diff in the tree) 18.508 against 18.475 (+0.2%): decode256k 39.1 /
39.0, prefill256k 2.0, serve256k1 32.98, serve256k3 81.17, decode 30.9, prefill128 10.5; identity serve256k-1
identical. Preflight 08:57:25: VM shut off, 3,521 MiB used, pressure avg10 0.00, no build tools. The gate has no
chunk under 256 rows (its prompts are 42-46 tokens, in windows): no harm shown, the gain is on the secondary
metric. `run.json` records correctness as skipped; the pass is `check_ds_log.py` on the three part logs of
772a7b95eae8.

Two more `serve256k` sessions on chunk156 (X58 k): `QX_DS_CHUNK_GRP_MAX=64` 35.5 / 80.52, `=0` 34.76 / 81.3, both
"identical" (three in all; these sessions contain no chunk: no harm, not evidence for the changed path). X103's

## 2026-11-16: A 1,024-row chunk is at the copy link and the disk (measurement only); the queue's items 2-4 closed by decision

nsys over a warmed 1,024-row chunk (X107, chunk156; 2,436 ms; the window from its first large host-to-device copy
to its last, 2,384 ms): 9,811 copies, 100.2 GB, the copy engine busy 2,238 ms (94% of the window, 44.8 GB/s; the
engine alone does 51); kernels busy 1,528 ms on 9 streams (expert kernels 918 ms in union, 4,200 ms summed: the
side streams overlap them); the card idle 18 ms. The lane: 7,808 records staged from RAM and 1,935 from NVMe
(1,582 ms waiting for NVMe reads: 18 GB at the disk's ~12 GB/s), 2,091 of 10,240 prefetch picks skipped; 10,142
experts used (253 a layer of 344).

So the three regimes are:
- **under 256 rows**: was the expert kernels (fixed cost an expert), now the copies (grouped kernels, landed today);
- **256 rows and up**: the copies at 94% duty and the disk beside them: the time is the bytes of the used experts
  outside VRAM (93 GB of ~96 GB used at 1,024 rows; the VRAM tier holds 13% of the records and a chunk takes its
  flex tail) over ~45 GB/s. The pool adds nothing (DMA 51 alone, 28 + 29 with the pool: one memory bus);
- **decode**: the same bus per token (findings).

Closed by decision (the queue of 2026-11-16):
- **Decode after a reused prompt** (+3 to +5% over 400 tokens against the same prompt afresh; +2,000 RAM
  evaluations): a fresh chunk resets the scores to the prompt's counts (its tick decays 2,700 tokens' worth at a
  half-life of 256) and promotes up to 512 at once. Re-applying a prompt's counts on reuse would buy at most ~0.5 s
  per 400-token reply against the 1.6-3.3 s the reuse saves, and costs copies of the same order. Under the bar.
- **Deep prefix reuse in one chunk** (the experts staged once, ~2 s): since the ring checkpoint a deep rewind
  happens only when history is edited 128+ tokens before a prompt's end, and is reused only from 8,192 tokens. A
  per-row replay flag through the chunk path (store writes, the per-layer trim, KV-only rows) for that path fails
  the complexity bar.
- **Three clients, decode at depth**: the profile of 2026-11-16 (X93) and the knob sweeps of 2026-11-15 stand.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-16/x-nsys-chunk1024/`.

## 2026-11-18: The user relaxed exactness on decode; residency-aware routing — KEPT as an opt-in knob, default off (decode256k -6% sustained at mean KL 0.010, top-1 96.4%)

The user's instruction of 2026-11-18: "Relax exactness on decode"; no hardware can be added (the spare 32 GB DIMMs
would make four on a two-channel board: no bandwidth, a lower DDR5 speed). Bound taken, the author's reading: the
prompt chunks' (mean KL <= 0.03, top-1 >= 95% against the exact path).

Lever: 38% of a decode256k step waits for experts outside VRAM (X93). `QX_DS_ROUTE_RES=m` (> 1): the router of
single steps and windows of at most `QX_DS_ROUTE_RES_T` tokens (default 8; never a chunk's slabs, never a replay)
scores an expert the VRAM tier holds as p * m + bias for the picks after the first `QX_DS_ROUTE_KEEP` (default 0);
the weights stay the true probs over the picks. `QX_DS_ROUTE_L0` / `_L1`: the layers it applies to.

Teacher-forced single steps from the 245,760-token state against the same binary's knob-off run (X109, X113;
logits of every row; ms per token over the whole run):

| setting | tokens | ms/token | mean KL | top-1 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| off | 1,024 / 4,096 | 38.9-39.8 / 37.9 | 0 | 100% |
| m 1.15, all layers | 1,024 | 34.8 | 0.0225 | 94.6% |
| m 1.3, all layers | 1,024 | 32.8 | 0.0376 | 94.6% |
| m 1.3, keep 3 | 1,024 | 32.3 | 0.0318 | 94.7% |
| m 1.3, layers 0-19 | 1,024 | 34.6 | 0.0340 | 94.5% |
| **m 1.3, layers 20-39** | 1,024 / 4,096 | 35.9 / **35.6 (-6.1%)** | 0.0114 / **0.0104** | 96.4% / **96.4%** |
| m 1.6, layers 20-39 | 1,024 | 34.4 | 0.0206 | 94.3% |
| m 3, layers 28-39 | 1,024 / 4,096 | 34.2 / 34.3 (-9.5%) | 0.0248 / 0.0288 | 95.6% / 95.2% |

- The cost is in the lower 20 layers: the same multiplier there costs 3.0x the KL for 1.85x the misses removed
  (10,873 RAM evaluations against 5,884).
- Binaries: X109 a 7cd417a02d98, X109 b and c 546be574dfac (both before the `res_ok` fix below); the 4,096-token
  rows (X113) ran the final engine, 126c35172bbd. Preflight of every chain: VM shut off.
- m 1.3 on layers 20-39 is stable over 4,096 tokens (per 512: KL 0.007-0.013, top-1 95-98%). m 3 on 28-39 drifts
  (KL 0.025 -> 0.039, top-1 97 -> 94%): rejected.
- Gate with m 1.3, L0 20 (X110 a, chunk158): decode256k 35.4 / 35.1 (baseline 39.0), decode 28.7 (31.0), serve256k3 73.64
  (80.84), score 17.315; not comparable to the scoreboard (lossy decode) and not promoted. The gain is larger
  in the first tokens after a resume (-9.6% over 64) than sustained (-6% over 4,096).
- Breaker (fresh context): (1) the knob reached the last slabs of exact chunks and replays through
  `layer_ffn_pre`: fixed (`res_ok`); (2) with the knob on, temperature-0 replies differ run to run (the router
  reads a table the driver writes asynchronously): the server identity check cannot pass, 8 of 8 replies differ
  between two knob-on sessions; (3) the tier scores the biased picks, so an expert the true router wanted and a
  resident displaced is never a promotion candidate (VRAM promotions -10%, RAM -18% at m 1.3 L0 20 over 4,096 tokens); (4) a prefix
  reused in place keeps lossy rows while the same prefix recomputed as a prompt is exact. Knob off is bit-identical
  by reading, by the server identity check (X111 a-1, a-3 on chunk158; x110-c on the final binaries) and by
  ds-checks a, b, c on the final engine binary (X118, 126c35172bbd: pass). `dsv41.cu` (the build's flattened
  source) compiles with nvcc for sm_86, sm_89, sm_120a (`x-route-res/nvcc-dsv41-archs.txt`: three exits 0).

Decision: opt-in, not the default. -6% does not pay for losing the identity check that guards every other change,
and (3) needs the true picks scored before it is trusted over a topic change. Recommended setting when wanted:
`QX_DS_ROUTE_RES=1.3 QX_DS_ROUTE_L0=20`.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-18/x-route-res/`, `x108-*`, `x110-a-203842`, `x111-a-*`.

## 2026-11-18: vLLM's throughput mechanisms surveyed; prompt-lookup speculation — default on for a sole decoder (a file edit 26.8 -> 18.8 s, a quotation 12.7 -> 9.2 s, exact); admission by shared prefix — default on

Survey (`~/git/vllm` at 34aac94f9c, fresh-context agent): of its mechanisms, the scheduler's decode-first token
budget, the per-batch CUDA graphs, the expert-grouped MoE kernels and the prefix cache have their counterpart here
(chunks beside decoders with a step gap, graph replay per width, the chunk kernels, slot prefix reuse with ring
checkpoints); async scheduling and the GPU-resident batch state hide host time this engine does not have (0.13 ms a
step). Ported: n-gram speculation (`spec_decode/ngram`), its batch-size-aware draft length, the per-request
acceptance, and cache-aware admission (`scheduler.py:2796`).

**Speculation** (`worker_ds.rs`; `QX_DS_SPEC=K`, default 15, 0 off; `QX_DS_SPEC_N` 4; `QX_DS_SPEC_COOL` 8;
`QX_DS_SPEC_MULTI` 0; `QX_DS_SPEC_N_MULTI` 8). A decoder whose last 4 tokens occurred before in its slot feeds
what followed them after its sample in the same window; the rows are sampled in turn while the samples equal the
drafts. After a rejection the slot is rewound (the free ring path, checked beforehand) to the group boundary
below and the token between is fed again. Draft length 2 -> 5 -> 11 -> 15 on full acceptance, the accepted count
after a rejection; a request sits 8 steps out after none accepted and 16 when under half were lately. Not under
a grammar, not in a step with prompt tokens, not within a group of the prompt's end.

Window costs today (X111 b, route1k): 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 tokens 30.8 / 45.4 / 76.8 / 133.6 ms a step.

| task (one client, temperature 0, 256k three-slot server) | off | K <= 7 | K <= 15 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| file edit, 695 tokens (X115) | 26.75 s | 19.63 | 18.75; final binary 18.89 (-29%) |
| quotation, 333 tokens | 12.65 s | 9.44 | 9.16; final binary 9.28 (-27%) |
| a reply that reasons before it edits, 500 tokens (X114) | 19.20 s | 18.86 | |
| JSON list, 400 tokens (X114) | 14.67 s | 14.95 (+1.9%) | not measured |
| essay, 300 tokens | 12.87 s | 13.00 (+1.0%) | not measured |

(The server's "generated in" times; at the client the edit is 29.26 -> 21.42 s and the quotation 15.27 -> 11.93 s.
The off, K <= 7 and K <= 15 columns ran chunk160, speculation before the later rules, one session each; "final
binary" is X121 on chunk164.)

Exactness: every reply byte-identical to speculation off (X114, X115, X120, X121); the server identity check
identical with it on (X111 c-1, X110 b, c); a recorded speculating session replayed as single steps
(`QX_DS_REPLAY_SPLIT=1`, X121): 212 forwards, 1,664 rows, 0 mismatching.

Beside other decoders it does not pay, so it is off there by default: with 3 drafts allowed a file edit beside
an essay went 30.9 -> 25.4 s and the essay 23.2 -> 29.4 s; two copy tasks beside an essay 22.9 -> 17.7-19.7 s and
the essay 25.3 -> 27.3 s (X120, chunk163); serve256k3 80.0-80.5 -> 83.7-84.2 ms per token at 15 drafts (X111 c, X110
b). A draft row costs a neighbour its share of a wider step.

Breaker (fresh context): tokens fed again could bring a step to 5 rows (2 + 2 + 1 with three decoders), a width
the sealed server never loaded: an abort. Fixed: decoders with such tokens sit a step out until the rows are a
width of `WIDTHS`. At the defaults the path cannot run with three slots (the group is 2, one token fed again, and
only a sole decoder speculates): it ran 24 times in a four-slot server with `QX_DS_SPEC_MULTI=3` (X124: three
copy tasks and an essay, replies identical to speculation off, no panic, widths 1-12 of `WIDTHS`). Sampling,
limits, stops, the checkpoint and the rewind path checked sound.

Final gate (x110-c-222808, chunk164: quixiinfer 6b1d46132e72, qx-ds-check 126c35172bbd; VM off): decode256k 39.2 /
38.8, prefill256k 2.0, serve256k3 80.78, decode 31.0, prefill128 10.5, identity identical, score 18.470 against
18.475: not a promotion. `perf_diff.py gate` prints REGRESSION for serve256k1 (35.54 against 32.98): the session
had 6 speculating steps (15 draft rows, 9 accepted), at most 0.2 s of 18.2; speculation-off sessions of the same
evening read 34.69, 37.16, 37.05, and 23 sessions of 11-15 / 11-16 read 32.88-37.66 (the baseline's is the fast
mode). The run's `run.json` says a clean tree: the binaries came from the worktree, whose diff is
`x-spec/candidate.diff` (sha256 68298c1882c2). ds-checks a, b, c (X118): pass, attached by hand. decode256k's
first repeat spent ~7 s once before its 64 tokens (174.5 ms a token over all 64 against 63-65): unexplained, outside
the last-32 figure.

Smoke and sampling (X125, chunk164, speculation on against off): a `json_object` request valid and identical; a
stop string inside a copied span, 49 tokens, identical; a sampled copy task (temperature 1.3, seed 7) identical on
and off and different from its greedy text; 393 of 428 drafts accepted. X122 (temperature 0.8) never left the
greedy path and shows nothing about sampling. The split replay of X121 first panicked (the tool needs
`QX_DS_CHUNK=8192` as the server has); the passing log is the re-run with it.

**Admission** (`QX_DS_ADMIT_WINDOW`, default 4; 1: arrival order): among the first 4 waiting requests the one
whose prefix a free slot would reuse (the admission's own value: the rings reach it, or 8,192+ tokens) goes
first; the front request is passed over three times at most. On the final binary (X116 w4f): one request
admitted out of order, 3,318 of 3,330 tokens reused, the batch 29.2 s, the second question 17.54 s, the short
request 22.19 s, replies identical to arrival order. X116 (chunk161, before the value rule; two requests
queued behind a busy document slot): the batch 32.4 -> 28.3 s, the second question on the document 25.1 -> 16.6 s
(3,318 of 3,330 tokens reused), the short request passed over 19.6 -> 21.3 s; replies identical. Which request
reuses a prefix decides which prompt runs in an f16 chunk: the existing prefix-reuse caveat, now also a matter of
queue order.

Second identity session and the short-context guard on the final binary (X111 d, e): serve256k identical,
serve256k1 35.56, serve256k3 81.04; serve1 34.19, serve3 77.46; no panic.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-18)
Routing knob: KEPT (opt-in, default off). Admission: LANDED. Speculation: HOLD until a smoke session (JSON mode,
a stop string in a copied span, sampling that leaves the greedy path) and a second identity session on the final
binary: both ran after the verdict (X125, X111 d, e above) and passed, so it lands on the adjudicator's stated
terms. Gate and scores reproduced (18.470; 18.718 and 17.315 for the earlier candidates); like for like and the
provenance chain verified; serve256k1's REGRESSION print judged not caused by the change (23 earlier sessions
32.88-37.66). Eighteen text corrections, applied.

Presuppositions attacked: 63 (decode must be exact), 64 (a decode step yields one token a request).
