## 2026-11-19: Suffix decoding — LANDED (the longest agreement in the slot's own history, default on; the corpus of other requests' replies KEPT opt-in); the tier scores the true picks under the routing knob — KEPT under the opt-in knob

Both asked for by the user on 2026-11-18. Candidate: `perf/results/2026-11-19/x-suffix-true/candidate.diff`
(binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk166`: quixiinfer 486c3078ae0b, qx-ds-check 5bd79a005c2f, qx-ds-replay e15e16efbdf4).
The win10 VM ran from 23:22:05 to 00:23:27 (the journal): the first X126 sessions (chunk165) and X113 b are
VM-on runs and are not used for times; ds-checks part a and most of b ran with it on (correctness only). At
23:57:19 systemd-oomd killed X126's first speculation-off server (memory pressure 62.65%); that arm was re-run.
Everything from X129 on has "vm shut off" in its preflight.

**Suffix decoding** (`worker_ds.rs`). `suffix_draft` replaces the most recent 4-token match: the place whose
preceding tokens agree with the sequence's end over the most tokens (at least `QX_DS_SPEC_N` 4, 24 counted), in
the slot's own history or in a corpus of finished requests' reply tokens (`QX_DS_SPEC_CORPUS`, default 0: off;
262,144 is the size tried; a place there must agree over `QX_DS_SPEC_N_CORPUS` 8), whichever agrees longer. The search stops
after 200,000 comparisons. A draft is still only a guess the window's rows confirm.

| task (one client, 256k three-slot server) | speculation off | chunk166 | note |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| file edit, 695 tokens (X129) | 27.12 s | 17.30 (-36%) | 18.89 with the most recent match (X121) |
| quotation, 333 tokens | 12.74 s | 8.76 (-31%) | 9.28 before |
| an essay asked again, 300 tokens (X126 on6 / off6, VM off) | 10.75 s | 6.35 (-41%) | 11.28 with the corpus off |
| a JSON list asked again, 400 tokens | 12.51 s | 6.49 (-48%) | 12.49 with the corpus off |
| the essay / the list / a reworded essay, first time (same sessions) | 11.94 / 14.87 / 10.98 s | 11.99 / 15.51 (+4.3%) / 10.95 | |
| essay, first time, 220 tokens (X129) | 8.88 s | 9.06 (+2.0%) | |
| the essay with one more instruction | 7.09 s | 7.11 | drafts from the corpus, rejected where it leaves |
| JSON list, 300 tokens / other field names | 11.95 / 8.92 s | 12.44 (+4.1%) / 8.99 | |
| essay, temperature 1.3, seed 7 | 8.57 s | 8.93 (+4.2%) | |

One session an arm. Prose with nothing to copy is within a single request's scatter (the first-time essay had one
speculating step in 221; `nocorp6` against `off6` differs by 5% on a request with one such step). What
reproduces is a first-time JSON list: +4.1% (X129), +4.3% (on6), +3.8% (nocorp6), with 58 of 118-126 drafts
accepted and no corpus step: drafts from its own history at half acceptance. Whether the longest agreement made
that worse than yesterday's +1.9% at K <= 7 is not isolated. Exactness: all seven replies of X129 and all five of X126 (chunk165 with the VM on, and
chunk166 with it off: on6, off6, nocorp6) byte-identical to speculation off; X129's session replayed as single steps: 1,276 forwards, 3,093 rows, 0 mismatching (33 steps drafted from
the corpus, 26 tokens fed again).

Breaker (fresh context; no result-corrupting defect): (1) the load generator's warm-up asks the prompts the serve
cases measure, so the corpus would draft every measured reply: `bench.sh` now starts its server with
`QX_DS_SPEC_CORPUS=0` (an experiment's own setting overrides it); (2) the search no longer stopped at the first
match: the comparison budget; (3) `QX_DS_SPEC_N` above 24 disabled speculation: clamped; (4) a full corpus agrees
over 4 tokens with almost anything: 8 for the corpus. Not measured: a full 262,144-token corpus (2.8 hours of
generation) on fresh text. A reply's speed can tell a client that another request produced the same continuation:
`QX_DS_SPEC_CORPUS=0` for a server with clients that must not learn that.

**True-pick scoring** (`dsv41.cu` `qx_ds_route` [11], `step.cu` `qx_moe_post` [21], `route_true_on`). With
`QX_DS_ROUTE_RES` on, the router also writes the picks by the true score; the post kernel lists those for the
tier's scoring and the driver takes the ones VRAM does not hold as promotion candidates. `QX_DS_ROUTE_TRUE=0`:
the selected ones, as before. 4,096 teacher-forced steps from 245,760, m 1.3 on layers 20-39 (X113 c, VM off):

| arm | ms/token | VRAM / RAM promotions | VRAM hits | NVMe | mean KL | top-1 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| knob off | 37.6 | 13,582 / 11,586 | 651,372 | 7,438 | 0 | 100% |
| old scoring | 35.7 | 12,158 / 9,579 | 690,387 | 6,123 | 0.0103 | 96.2% |
| true picks | 35.6 | 13,576 / 11,621 | 695,443 | 5,622 | 0.0102 | 96.6% |

The tier adapts as with the knob off again, at the same speed and KL. What it buys over a topic change is not
measured. With the knob on, every job's selected experts outside VRAM are candidates too (the switch is global).
A 256k three-slot server with the knob, speculation windows and three clients (X120 route): no panic.
Kernel info words are not zero-padded by the launcher: every `qx_ds_route` call passes 12 words, `qx_moe_post` 22
(the older engine's call padded with zeros; that engine was not run).

Knob off: ds-checks a, b, c on 5bd79a005c2f (X127): pass. `dsv41.cu` and `step.cu` compile for sm_86, sm_89,
sm_120a (`nvcc-archs.txt`). Gate (x110-d-005348, chunk166, VM off, the corpus off in the server): decode256k 38.9
/ 39.5, prefill256k 2.0, serve256k3 80.52, decode 31.2, prefill128 10.5, identity identical, score 18.482 against
18.475: not a promotion. serve256k1 37.19 prints REGRESSION against 32.98: the slow mode of that case again (6
speculating steps in the session; 34.69-37.16 with speculation off on 2026-11-18).

Second and third identity sessions on the final binary (chunk167: quixiinfer ba327ac433b3, the corpus's default
set to 0, nothing else; X111 f, VM off): default: identical, serve256k1 34.55, serve256k3 80.58; with
`QX_DS_SPEC_CORPUS=262144`: identical, 38 steps drafted from other replies (462 of 462 drafts accepted), and
serve256k1 reads 19.02: the warm-up's replies replayed, as the breaker said, which is why the gate's server runs
with the corpus off (the settings string of `run.json` does not carry that; the command line does). The gate
x110-d ran chunk166 with the corpus off in the server: one identity session; preflight 00:53:48, memory used

correctness skipped (ds-checks attached by hand), `experiment_env QX_X=1` (read by nothing).

True picks also read VRAM hits +0.7% and NVMe reads -8% against the old scoring (X113 b and c); the equal
promotion counts follow partly from the larger candidate set.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-19)
Gate, like for like, provenance, ds-checks and the true-pick table reproduced; serve256k1's print is the case's
slow mode (37.19 inside the 32.88-37.66 of 23 earlier sessions). Suffix decoding in the slot's own history: HOLD

## 2026-11-19: Speculation's standing kept per kind of draft (source x agreement length) — LANDED, default on, not a promotion (a first-time JSON list +3 to +5% -> -0.1 to +1.0%; the corpus on fresh tasks +2.3% -> -0.8%; a list with another reply's values under other keys 0 -> +3 to +4%)

Asked for by the user ("fix the first-time JSON slowdown with per-source acceptance tracking"). Candidate:
`perf/results/2026-11-19/x-spec-kinds/candidate.diff` (`worker_ds.rs` only; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk174`,
quixiinfer f319384fef1b; the engine binary is unchanged, 5bd79a005c2f). VM shut off in every preflight.

Before: one acceptance rate, one draft length and one sit-out a request. Now four kinds of draft, each with its own
record (`SpecSrc`): the slot's own history or the corpus, and an agreement under 16 tokens or of 16 and more. A
kind's record is what its steps were worth in draft rows (an accepted token 2, or 1.33 beside other decoders; a
draft row and the token fed again 1; a rejection 2), 0.7 of the old value and 0.3 of the last step's. Under zero:
the kind sits out `QX_DS_SPEC_COOL` steps, twice as long each time in a row (256 at most; forgotten only above
2, which drafts of 2 cannot reach; the code's comment says "a short draft's best is 2", wrong for agreements of
6-15 tokens, whose drafts are 3-7), and starts again from 2 drafts. A short agreement earns a draft of half its
length; the next length grows from the one accepted.

Why four and not two (the builds tried, one session each; "off" is speculation off):

| build | first-time JSON, 400 tokens (off 14.97) | first-time JSON, 300 (off 11.95-12.04) | a list with other keys (off 8.92-8.98) | JSON then a file copied, 1,091 tokens (off 41.87) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| chunk168, before (12.44 and 8.99 are chunk166's, the same counters; X126 def8 on chunk168: 15.66) | 15.42, 15.66 | 12.30, 12.44 | 8.91, 8.99 | 30.01 |
| chunk169: per source, a rate rule | 15.66 | | | |
| chunk170: + draft by agreement length, sticky doubling | 15.00 | 12.12, 12.13 | 8.92, 8.94 | 34.18 (the source locked out when the copy began) |
| chunk171: + a long agreement ends a sit-out | | 12.19 | 9.36 | 29.54 |
| chunk172: per kind, forgiving doubling | 14.98 | 12.60 | 9.30 | 29.20 |
| chunk173: per kind, sticky doubling | 14.94 | 12.17 | 9.23 | 29.29 |
| **chunk174: + a rejection counts 2** | **14.96** | **12.09, 12.16** | 9.24, 9.27 | **29.44** |

On chunk174 against speculation off in the next session (X129 n174 / off174): essay 8.94 / 8.89 s, the essay with
one more instruction 7.07 / 7.10, JSON list 12.09 / 12.04 (+0.4%), the list with other keys 9.24 / 8.98 (+2.9%),
essay at temperature 1.3 8.90 / 8.65 (+2.9%), file edit 17.02 / 27.25 (-38%), quotation 8.70 / 12.75 (-32%).
Asked again (X126 n174): essay 6.35 s, JSON list 6.66 against 6.49 / 6.50 on chunk168 (37 forwards against 35:
+2.6%, the same on chunk172-174).
The corpus with 11,700 tokens and three fresh tasks (X131): n174b 11.35 + 10.57 + 12.42 = 34.34 s and n174 34.14
(during one of the two the author launched `quixiinfer serve --help`, which started the default engine and died of

11.45). Not strictly held out: chunk171 read 34.56 before chunk172-174 were built.

What the change costs: a list that agrees long with another reply and fails at each key was at parity before
(8.91, 8.99 against 8.92-8.98) and reads 9.24, 9.27 now (+2.9 to +3.9%); a sampled essay +2.9 to +4.8% (+0.6 to

## 2026-11-19: The RAM tier's elastic part (`QX_DS_RAM_YIELD`): the service hands 12 GiB back to the host while the win10 VM runs and takes them again after — LANDED, off by default, on in production (gate score unchanged off and on; 12 GiB out: score +5.4%, replies identical)

Why: the user's VM (16 GiB, 16 vCPUs, started now and then for a build) and the service (104.5 GB resident, swap
off for the unit, the expert tier pinned) do not fit a 120 GiB box together: 19.3 GiB are available with the service alone. A test
server was killed by systemd-oomd beside the VM on 11-18. Wanted: the VM costs speed while it runs, nothing else.

What: with `QX_DS_RAM_YIELD=G` the coldest G GiB of the RAM tier's budget get their places in a second pinned
arena per size class (`tiers.rs` `build_elastic`). `DsEngine::ram_yield(true)`, called by the scheduler between
steps, has the driver thread (`yield_check`) wait for every copy and promotion read, return the quarantined
places, keep the hottest records in the first arenas (memcpy of those that sat in the elastic ones), drop the
coldest to NVMe-only, free the arenas and update the GPU's RAM table before it acknowledges. `ram_yield(false)`
pins the arenas again (not zeroed) and `promote_one` reads the dropped records back, hottest first, turn about
with the adaptive promotions. The trigger (`worker_ds.rs` `Yielder`, five looks a second, also while idle): out
while `QX_DS_YIELD_PATH` exists and, if it holds a pid, that process lives (the VM's pid file: there before the
guest touches memory), or while MemAvailable is under `QX_DS_YIELD_LOW` (4 GiB); back once the path is gone and
MemAvailable has stayed above the part plus `QX_DS_YIELD_HIGH` (8 GiB, at least LOW + 2) for `QX_DS_YIELD_HOLD`
(20 s). A server started beside the path builds the store with the part out. Unset: one arena per class, the
free list in its old order.

Measured (VM off, the service stopped; raw in `perf/results/2026-11-19/x-ram-yield/`). Builds: chunk175 the first
version; chunk176 the restore not zero-filled; chunk177 the breaker's fixes; chunk178 (quixiinfer 7f139cfb47dd,
qx-ds-check ee12685b9263; the engine as in chunk177) a warning silenced. What counts is on chunk178 unless said.
- Gate (all three on chunk178). Variable unset (x110-f-122224): 18.468 against 18.475, identity identical. Set and
  in (x110-g-123004): 18.470, identical; its unscored serve256k1 reads 35.68 and prints REGRESSION against 32.98
  (f: 33.21), inside the case's known two levels (34.1-37.2). Set and out for the whole run (x110-h-123741, a pid
  file naming pid 1): 19.471 (+5.4%): decode256k 42.3, 42.0 (39.0), serve256k3 87.57 (80.8), serve256k1 37.24,
  decode 32.0 (30.8), prefill128 10.9 (10.5), prefill256k 2.0; identical. That is the price while the VM is up,
  before whatever its 16 vCPUs take from the pool. The gates' sessions release and restore nothing.
- ds-checks on ee12685b9263 (X144, parts a, b, c; `ds-checks.log`): pass, each part the same text as the X86 run.
- Times (all builds): a release 0.51-0.72 s when few records move, 1.1-1.3 s when most do (1,252 of 1,263); a
  restore 1.56-1.69 s (2.06 s zero-filled, chunk175); the engine thread waits that long, once each. The host has
  the memory within a second, idle or not (X141, chunk175: 19.2 -> 21.8 -> 31.4 GiB at 0.5 s steps; the service's
  own line two seconds after each release on chunk178: 31.5-31.6 GiB available, 19.4 before). The refill's
  duration is not timed; on an idle server its last line follows the restore within seconds.
- Replies on chunk178, each byte-identical to the same request before any yield. X140 c: short prompts out, back,
  with the release mid-reply (the restore came after the reply), after two more releases of which one came before
  the refill was over (584 records), and after a release by a pid file the service cannot read: 11 of 11, five
  releases and restores. X142 b, prefix reuse off so every prompt runs the chunk path (chunk steps 26): a 12.5k-token
  prompt out, refilling, and after the flapping (3 of 3 beside a control), three prompts at once out and with the
  path coming and going twice while they ran (6 of 6 beside 3 controls); three releases (1,040, 871, 46 moved),
  the third 71 records into a refill.
- On earlier builds, same results: X140 a (chunk175, 10 of 10; its waits matched the store's start-up line, so
  its phases ran early and two waits timed out: the server did the right thing, three releases); X140 b
  (chunk176): a 12.5k-token prompt asked again out and back, decode 4.25 / 4.24, 4.19 / 4.16, 4.17 s, released
  by a 13 GiB bytearray at LOW=8 (out at 7.0 GiB available, back 5 s after it left; resident 90.1 G out and
  102.1 G just after the return against 104.5: not looked into); X142 a (chunk176) ended without its last prompt
  and without its summary line: the chain's `wait` in a subshell never returned (17 CPU-minutes) while the
  server sat idle and healthy until its 420 s limit; with explicit pids (X142 b) it ran through.
- Bytes (`tier-check-elastic.txt`, chunk178: qx-tier-check with QX_TIER_ELASTIC=8 on a 30 GiB budget): every
  record in RAM compared with the source GGUF after build, after churn (600 evictions, 3,000 promotion
  candidates), after each release (417-504 moved, 630-895 dropped), after each refill, three rounds, and for a
  store built out and then restored: 0 mismatches in 12 of 12 stages, no two records in one place. This checks
  the store alone, not the driver's table words, copies or quarantine.
- Started beside the path (X143 b, chunk177): 8,327 records (79.0 GiB), 92.5 G resident; path gone: 104.5 G and
  the same reply. A pid file naming no process: no release in 4 s. Twenty releases and restores idle: resident
  104.5 -> 104.5 G, available 19.4 -> 19.3 GiB, GPU 30,927 -> 30,927 MiB. (X143 a stopped after one release:
  its wait helper returned failure and left the path in place; the server rightly stayed out. X143 b printed
  one warning, "19.3 GiB available 2 s after the release": the part was back by then at HOLD=1; chunk178 says
  nothing in that case.)

Breaker (fresh context, reading only): no path found that reads or writes a freed place. Its points and what
became of them: the memcpy branch must have run (it did: 1,251, 1,042, 867 moved); reply text is a weak detector
for cold records (hence the byte check above); the free list's order had changed with the variable unset (put
back); a stale pid file kept the part out for good (pid checked); HIGH below LOW could flap (clamped); a start
beside the VM pinned everything (built out); the refill ran ahead of the adaptive promotions (turn about).

Not covered:
- The real VM, which I may not start. That its pid file exists before the guest touches memory is libvirt's
  convention, not something seen here; the file's mode is unknown (both readable and unreadable are handled).
  The first real start is the test, and the journal's `ram tier:` lines say what happened.
- The release waits for the scheduler's current step: up to one prompt chunk (8,192 tokens at 2.0 ms: 16 s).
  Whether the VM's 16 GiB plus qemu's own fit the 19.3 GiB available for that long is not measured; a test
  server was killed beside the VM on 11-18 (its settings then are not on record).
- The low mark was tested at 8 GiB, not at the default 4.
- `QX_DS_GPU_SHARE`, `QX_DS_PREFETCH`, `QX_DS_STG_WAIT=words` (off by default; the breaker's "no path reads a
  freed place" is a reading of the code, and for these three nothing more).
- `QX_DS_ROUTE_RES` (off by default): with it a release changes which records are resident and so the replies.
- The refill's cost to a loaded server's promotions (turn about) is not measured; its correctness under load is
  X142 b's flapping phase.
- `ram_yield` gives the driver 120 s to answer, then panics (the unit restarts, built out if the VM is up).
- `QX_DS_ASYNC=0` (no driver) prints the store's line and never yields.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-19): HOLD, then lifted
Gate arithmetic, identity, like for like and every reply comparison reproduced. HOLD for: no ds-checks on the
binaries; the release and restore evidence taken on builds before the final one; X142 a's end unexplained; a list
of numbers and claims stronger than the logs. Answered by X144 (ds-checks pass), X140 c and X142 b on chunk178,
and the corrections above. The verdict's own condition: commit before the production install.

## 2026-11-19: Image input (the model's vision tower) — LANDED (a feature, not a promotion; without images the scored cases and identity unchanged)

Why: the user: "implement vision support ... mandatory". The model is image-text-to-text and ships its projector
(`mmproj-F16.gguf`); the server dropped image parts silently and the model guessed.

What, from the model's own reference (`inference/vision.py`, `image_processor.py`, `model.py`, `engram.py`;
copies in `~/qx-ref-vision/`):
- `crates/qx-vision`: the tower on the CPU in f32. Fitting as PIL's `ImageOps.pad` (Pillow's fixed-point bicubic,
  grey 127), a 32-block ViT (width 1,024, RMSNorm, SwiGLU, 2D rotary positions), the aligner (3x3 unfold, two
  linears with an erf GELU), the block `[START] ([IMAGE] x w [NEWLINE]) x h [END]`, at most 1,024 positions.
- Engine: an image position is a token id `IMG_BASE + serial` (0x4000_0000 on). `image_ids` keeps the blocks' rows
  in a host-mapped table (QX_DS_IMG_ROWS, 16,384 rows, 335 MB pinned; the least recently used block that nothing
  names leaves, then those only idle slots' contexts name, whose slots are reset). At upload an id becomes bit 31
  plus its table row. Kernels: `qx_dequant_*` decodes row 0 for such an id, `qx_ds_hc_init` takes the table row
  as the embedding, `qx_ds_engram_gate` skips the position (gate 0), `qx_ds_route` takes `exp_probs_b_vl`. The
  Engram's hash of later tokens sees ids outside its token map as dead already.
- Server: image parts (data: URLs, Anthropic `image`) in template order; one placeholder token per image expanded
  to its positions; the encode in a task of its own (one at a time, 16 unpinned threads, its result cached whether
  or not the client waits; the last blocks kept by recency, 335 MB); the scheduler registers the blocks at intake,
  before prefixes are compared. Bearer check before the body is read; body limit 96 MB; a projector of another
  width refused at start. `--mmproj FILE` (default: `mmproj-F16.gguf` in or above the model's directory).

Measured (VM off, the service stopped; raw in `perf/results/2026-11-19/x-vision/`). Final build chunk185
(quixiinfer 0a8351be6436, qx-ds-check 81e0755692eb); earlier builds named where they are the evidence.
- The tower against `tools/vision_ref.py` (NumPy + Pillow, written separately from the same reference): fitted
  pixels byte-identical to Pillow on six PNGs (small, mid, tall, wide, exact multiple, RGBA; 200x150 to
  3000x700); block values max |d| 1.5e-5 and 3.7e-5, row cosine 0.9999998 (194 and 206 rows; the two large images
  were compared for pixels only). A JPEG: 83,177 bytes differ from Pillow's decode, by at most 4 levels.
- Image positions in the engine, numerically (`qx-ds-img-check`, 12 text tokens + 40 block rows + 12 text tokens,
  single steps with dumps): embedding = the block's row in every stream, bit for bit, 40 of 40; Engram gates all
  zero at image positions (0 wrong of 80 position-layers) and at no text position-layer; router picks = the top 6
  under `exp_probs_b_vl` at 1,600 of 1,600 image position-layers (the text bias would pick differently at 1,452)
  and under `exp_probs_b` at 960 of 960 text ones; windows of 8 and one exact chunk give the single steps' logits
  after the last token bit for bit.
- Answers through the API (greedy, thinking off), all correct: solid red / blue / green; "ZEBRA 58213" read
  exactly; 3 squares counted; two and three images named in order; a label read in a scene, as PNG and JPEG, and
  in a 1,014-position and a 977-position image; an image asked again (1,028 of 1,030 tokens reused, 0.6 s), a
  second turn (0.7 s) and the same streamed; an image request beside a decoding one; three image prompts at once
  with cached blocks ("chunk steps 5 (1 of several slots)"); 3,273 tokens with the image at the start; windows
  only (QX_DS_CHUNK_MIN=100000: red, the text, the 1,014-position image, 0 chunk steps); after evictions with a
  600-row table (red, text, squares, green, blue, red again, text again: all right; a 1,014-position image and
  four images over 600 rows refused with a 400).
- Real pictures (X153, chunk185): five of Ubuntu's 3840-pixel wallpapers (836 KB to 3.6 MB, JPEG and PNG) each
  described in a sentence that fits its file name (bluebells on a forest path, a red acer leaf, a coffee mug with
  the Ubuntu logo, a raccoon illustration, a starry sky over the sea); a logo read as "Ubuntu"; from a rendered
  invoice of eight lines the number, total and reference code exactly. Each large picture is fitted to 968-1,001
  positions and took 21.9-26.9 s to encode while the adjudicator's own CPU checks ran beside it (19.3-23.5 s
  otherwise): that, not 2 s, is what a large photograph costs. Gist-level answers: no real photograph was
  tested for small text, charts or counting.
- Server behaviour: 401 without the key (the check is a layer in front of the body's extraction: by the code;
  the request carried a 58 KB image); a 12 MB noise image accepted and answered ("Random", 1,011 prompt tokens,
  X154 main; on chunk183/184 it only passed the body limit, the 600-row table refused it);
  nine distinct images twice: 9 encodes, then 0; a client giving up after 6 s three times on a new large image:
  one encode, then the answer in 3.9 s; bad base64, an http URL, a non-image: 400 each.
- Cost: encode 2.1-2.7 s for 184-218 positions, 19.3-23.5 s for 947-1,014 (16 threads beside the engine's
  pool). An essay decoding beside a 19 s encode (947 positions): 22.2 tok/s against 28.3 alone. Resident
  106.4-106.7 GB (peak 107.3-107.5, reached at start and not raised by any encode) against 104.5: the tower's f32
  weights (1.9 GB) and the table. Available beside the service: 16.4-17.0 GiB (19.3 before).
- Without images: gate x110-j-172148 on chunk185 18.351 against 18.475 (decode256k 39.1, 39.2; serve256k3
  78.93; decode 31.2; prefill128 10.5; prefill256k 2.0), identity identical. `perf_diff.py gate` exits 1 on the
  unscored serve256k1: 36.01 against 32.98 prints REGRESSION; the case read 33.2-37.2 over today's sessions on
  every build (its two known levels). `run.json` records correctness "skipped": the ds-checks are attached by
  hand. A second session on chunk185 (x111-v-1-180731): identical, serve256k1 35.86, serve256k3 81.17.
  x110-i-162914 on chunk182 (the same kernels) 18.578, identical. ds-checks on chunk185 (X146, parts a, b, c; `ds-checks.log`): pass, each part the same text as the X86 run; the
  image-position check on chunk185 (`img-check.log`): the figures above, ok.
  The changed kernel files (`dsv41.cu`, `gemv.cu`) compile with `nvcc -cubin` for sm_86, sm_89 and sm_120a
  (`nvcc-archs.txt`).
- Reruns on chunk185 after the adjudicator's HOLD (X154): two and three images in one request; two turns and
  the same streamed (1,006 and 992 tokens reused; turn 2 in 0.7 s); the three 400s; the 600-row table (red,
  text, squares, green, blue, red again, text again: all right; 1,014 and 756 positions refused before anything
  is displaced; red right after the refusals); and production's settings (the profile, the corpus on,
  QX_DS_RAM_YIELD=12 with a pid path): an image answered with the part in, out (a cached image, a new one, and
  a 1,014-position prefix reused) and back; resident 106.6 -> 94.6 -> 106.6 G, available 17.0 -> 28.7 -> 16.7.

Breaker (fresh context, reading only, on the build before the fixes): no path found that corrupts results; every
call site of the three changed kernels passes every word they read; no other reader of the token array. Its
defects, all answered: idle contexts pinned the table (they give way now); abandoned requests piled up encodes
(own task, cached); the cache was first-in-first-out of eight (by recency and size); bodies were parsed before
the key was checked (a layer in front); a projector of another width crashed the scheduler (refused at start).
Its tests: the numeric check (written: `qx-ds-img-check`), the window path, three truly at once, text after an
image beyond the kept tail, eviction and re-registration: run, above.

Not covered:
- A deep rewind whose replay chunk crosses an image: the scheduler chose a fresh prefill both times it was
  set up (X152 b, d: 0 reused). The replay uses the same per-row ids as the chunk the numeric check covers.
- f16 prompt chunks on image rows are held to no measured bound (the KL bound was measured on text); the answers
  above went through them. KV-only rows over an image: the 3,273-token prompt is the only case.
- `qx-ds-replay` cannot replay a session with images (the trace holds ids, not blocks). The f32 CPU reference
  (`qx-ref`) has no image path, so ds-checks do not see image positions; `qx-ds-img-check` is its own check.
- The predictors and the prefetch use the text bias at image positions (order of expert staging, not results).
- Image URLs are not fetched. Images above 20 MB or more than 16 per request are refused. A request that fails
  at its k-th image has already displaced idle contexts for the earlier ones.
- `tools/vision_ref.py` is by the same author from the same reference: it shows the Rust tower matches that
  reading of `vision.py` and that mapping of the GGUF's tensors, not that the reading is right. The independent
  evidence is behavioural (exact text from 8x23 and 44x22 grids, the invoice, the photographs). llama.cpp's
  port (`tools/mtmd/clip.cpp`, built here as a library) was read but not run against it; torch is not installed,
  so the reference itself was never run.
- Fitting differs from Pillow for 16-bit PNG (Pillow clips, the `image` crate scales) and by up to 4 levels
  for JPEG (the decoder); the effect on the block is not measured. EXIF orientation is not applied and alpha is
  dropped (both as the reference does). HEIC, AVIF and TIFF are refused; an animated GIF gives its first frame.
- Encodes are serial across all clients: a request with 16 photographs holds the tower for about 6 minutes.
- What image traffic does to the expert tiers (image positions route under another bias) and to the speed of
  text decode afterwards is not measured.
- The vision threads are not kept off the engine's cores (the 22 against 28 tok/s above); the tower on the GPU
  (the f16 GEMM kernel exists) is the way to seconds.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-19): HOLD, then lifted
Scored gate, identity, ds-checks' binary, the chain-to-build mapping and every quoted reply reproduced; the tower
compared again with `vision_ref.py` on one image (3.7e-5). HOLD for: an uncommitted tree with an untracked check;
one identity session on the final server; five user-facing paths last run before the table and server rewrites;
claims stronger than their logs (the title, the 12 MB upload, the 0.9 GB, the 44 KB body, the 23 s encode, the
compile without a log) and gaps in "Not covered"; and a smoke under production's settings before the deploy.
Answered by X154 (the five paths and production's settings with a hand-over), x111-v (identical), `nvcc-archs.txt`
and the corrections above. Its cadence flag stands: the last four experiments attacked no presupposition; the
next one must.

## 2026-11-19: The vision tower on the GPU — LANDED (a large photograph's encode 19-24 s -> 0.5 s; without images the scored cases and identity unchanged)

Why: the user: "we need to optimize it". The CPU tower took 2-3 s for a small picture and 19-24 s for a photograph,
one image at a time across all clients, on threads that share the engine's cores.

What: `crates/qx-engine/src/vision_gpu.rs` (`GpuTower`) and `kernels/vision.cu`. The matrix products are the
engine's `qx_gemm16` (f16 operands, f32 accumulation); per block of query rows the attention is three launches for
all 16 heads (scores as 16 groups, a softmax, the probabilities' product with the values in K slices summed by
`qx_gemm16_sum`); norms, the rotary rotation, SwiGLU, the unfold and the GELU are small kernels. The tower has a
host thread and a low-priority stream of its own; its info words go through a ring of its own, copied once per
batch of launches. Weights stay in pinned host memory and are streamed into a 26 MB staging area layer by layer.
325 MB of device memory, allocated after the engine's warm-up out of what it leaves free (1.21 GiB), refused if
less than 512 MB would remain (`QX_VISION_HEADROOM_MIB`); the expert tier gives up nothing. Probabilities are
stored in f16 times 4,096 (most are below f16's normal range otherwise). The CPU tower stays as the fallback
(no room, `QX_VISION=cpu`, a block with a value that is not finite, a driver error); its 1.9 GB of f32 weights are
now read only if it is used.

Measured (VM off, the service stopped; raw in `perf/results/2026-11-19/x-vision-gpu/`). Final build chunk191
(quixiinfer def2c5bcefdf, qx-ds-check 8f1603b13033).
- Encode alone (`qx-vision-gpu-check`, `gpu-check.txt`): 0.058-0.059 s for 189-206 positions, 0.48-0.51 s for
  968-1,014 (CPU tower beside it: 2.2-2.7 s and 22.0-23.1 s). The tool's pass bound was loosened after the first
  measurement, from a row cosine above 0.999 to above 0.97 with a mean difference under 1%: its "ok" is against
  a bound set knowing the numbers. Two runs give the same bits on all five images.
- Against the CPU tower: mean |d| / mean |x| 0.02%, 0.33%, 0.24%, 0.21%, 0.12%; lowest row cosine 1.000000,
  0.998369, 0.980934 (tall.png), 0.994541, 0.999715. `tools/vision_ref.py` with `VISION_REF_F16=1` (the same
  rounding points in NumPy) on mid.png: lowest rows 63, 201, 137 at 0.99809, 0.99946, 0.99969; the GPU tower's
  lowest row there is 0.998369. The largest activation fed to a product in that run: 804.5 (f16 holds 65,504).
  The 0.981 of tall.png was not reproduced in NumPy (not run).
- In the server (X160 c, prefix reuse off so every prompt is prefilled anew): every image question of the CPU
  tower's tests answered with the same content (not always the same words: the squares came back as "3" on
  chunk191 and as a sentence on chunk190, so the tower's bits changed between the two builds) (colours, "ZEBRA 58213", 3 squares, three images, the 1,014- and
  977-position labels, the invoice's three fields, the logo, five photographs). 56 encodes, all on the GPU; in
  the server a large image encodes in 0.50-0.65 s. A request with a photograph answers in 3.7-4.2 s (25.6-31.4 s
  before), one with a small picture in 1.9-2.7 s (3.9-4.8 s). Thirty new small images one after another: 42.9 s.
- The engine beside the tower: a 12.5k-token text prompt, prefilled anew, gives the same reply alone and while six
  requests with new large images run (13.9 s alone, 27.2 s beside them: those six also prefill 1,000 positions
  each). An essay decodes at 28.3 tok/s alone and 9.1 beside six image requests in a row; beside one (X161
  prod2) 22.2 against 25.3: per second the cost to a neighbour is what it was with the CPU tower (22.2 against
  28.3), but for about 3 s instead of 21.
- Beside the engine's windows with prompt chunks off (X162 a; `x162-a.server-full.log`): one lazy graph capture
  and 77 KV-only windows fell inside the traffic of ten new 1,014-position images (the other 14 captures are the
  warm-up's, before the tower exists; nothing shows the one overlapped an encode in time): ten codes read exactly, no panic,
  no encode given up, GPU memory 31,267-31,281 MiB throughout.
- Production's settings with a RAM-tier hand-over (X161 prod2): images answered with the part in, out and back;
  resident 105.7 -> 93.7 -> 105.7 G (was 106.6-106.7 with the CPU tower's weights loaded), GPU 31,269-31,283 MiB,
  18.1 GiB available beside the service (16.4-17.0 before). `QX_VISION=cpu` (X161 cpu2, chunk191): the CPU tower
  answers as before (3.3 s encode). `QX_VISION_HEADROOM_MIB=2000` (X163, chunk191): the tower is refused at start
  ("would have 909 MB free"), nothing stays allocated (GPU 30,943 MiB) and the CPU tower answers.
- Without images: gate x110-n-220838 on chunk191: 18.447 against 18.475 (decode256k 38.9, 39.0; serve256k3
  80.58; decode 31.0; prefill128 10.5; prefill256k 2.0), identity identical. The unscored serve256k1 reads 37.43
  against 32.98 and prints REGRESSION (gate exit 1); it is just above the 33.2-37.2 the case has shown today on
  every build. `run.json` records correctness "skipped": ds-checks attached by hand (X148, 8f1603b13033: pass,
  each part the same text as X86) with `qx-ds-img-check` (ok). x110-k-193406 on chunk190: 18.355, identical.
  x110-m-202015 (20.145) is void: the NumPy experiment ran on the CPU beside it.
- `kernels/vision.cu` compiles with `nvcc -cubin` for sm_86, sm_89 and sm_120a (`nvcc-archs.txt`).

Breaker (fresh context, reading only, on chunk190): no layout or offset defect. Its points and the answers:
the info ring wrapped inside one encode (39,626 launches against 32,768 slots; the large images passed on
timing): now 11,532 launches for the largest image tried (`profile-tall.txt`; the breaker's 39,626 is its own count of
  the old code) in a ring of 65,536 that restarts with each encode, asserted; calls that CUDA
forbids beside another thread's graph capture (stream synchronize, pageable copies): every transfer is pinned and
the end is an event polled, and X162 ran it with one lazy capture in the traffic (the rule holds by construction, with that one instance); headroom unchecked: checked; no finiteness check
on the server path: added, with the CPU tower behind it; a panic poisoned the tower for good: errors return and a
poisoned lock is taken over; a warm-up over 300 s chose the CPU tower silently: it waits; leaks and missing type
checks in `new`: everything is read and checked before the first allocation. Its "identical beside six encodes"
objection (the second prompt had been reused, not prefilled): rerun with reuse off, above.

Not covered:
- The GPU tower differs from the CPU tower (rows down to cosine 0.981). f16 rounding is shown to account for it
  on mid.png only; for tall.png's 0.981 it is the attribution, not shown. No accuracy benchmark on either tower.
- No fresh-context reading of chunk191's layout (the block size, buffers serving two purposes, the aligner in
  parts, the pinned buffers changed after the breaker read chunk190): its evidence is numerical, the five images
  and the server's answers. The non-finite fallback, the driver-error return and the poisoned-lock take-over
  have never run.
- serve256k1 read 35.87 and 37.43 on the two GPU-tower builds: the upper half of the case's range, one sample each.
- sm_86 / sm_89: compiled, never run (and `qx_gemm16` needs tensor cores those cards have, but nothing here ran
  on them).
- Image traffic still slows text clients: the image's 1,000 prompt positions and the tower share the GPU and the
  context with decode (9.1 against 28.3 tok/s under back-to-back image requests). Not attacked here.
- A deep rewind across an image, f16 prompt chunks on image rows, the tiers under image traffic: as before.
- 891-901 MB of device memory stay free with the tower; the engine's lazy allocations fitted in every run here
  (14 MiB of growth at most), but that is these runs.

### Adjudication (fresh context, 2026-11-19): LANDED
Gate (18.447), identity (two sessions on the final server), the ds-checks' binary, the preflight and the voiding
of x110-m reproduced. Corrections applied above (one lazy capture, not 15; request times before; the loosened
bound; what rests on which build); the CPU switch and the headroom refusal run on chunk191 at its suggestion.
The cadence flag stands: the next experiment must attack a presupposition.

Raw: `perf/results/2026-11-19/x-vision-gpu/`, `x110-n-220838`, `x110-k-193406`.
